# 16 — Munich rig test protocol: what was measured on 2026-09-01, and how to run the next days

**Read this before touching the rig.** It supersedes the operational half of
[15](15_RIG_WINDOW_RUNBOOK.md) (bring-up, safety, choreography) and records the
site facts that [12 §2](12_INFERENCE_PATH_NOTE.md) listed as "confirm on the
robot, do not assume". 15 §1.2 (parity gate), §2 (evaluation protocol) and §3
(decision tree) are unchanged and still govern scoring. Everything here was
measured on site on **2026-09-01, 16:14–18:00 CEST**, from the MacBook over
`ebim-franka` (`ssh ebim@192.168.0.5`), with the operator at the robot.

Two rules carry over from AGENTS.md and are the reason this document is a table
of rows rather than a narrative: **the summary lies, not the measurement** — every
verdict below is re-derivable from the rows it sits on — and **a passing check is
not a working feature**. Where a fact is *not* measured it is marked
**UNMEASURED** in §6, not quietly assumed.

## 0. State of play (end of 2026-09-01)

**What works, proven by the sine probe (§3, run 2 in R-30):** a camelo
`CommandPublisher` in the `camelo-ros` container on the station publishes
`/right/gello/joint_states`; the companion's `joint_impedance_controller`
accepts it, activates, and the **right arm FOLLOWED a ±0.03 rad sine on
joints 6–7 from t = 20.5 s (first measurable displacement; both controllers
activated at t ≈ 19.5 s, the motion-generator approach runs in between) until
Ctrl+C at t = 280 s** (max
|measured offset| 0.0499 / 0.0399 rad on j6 / j7), the **left arm held at
0.0000 rad**, both arms' `measured_joint_states` arrived in the container at
~700 Hz, and the arm stack logged **zero** fault lines before, during and after.
Companion → container state (arms, grippers, wrenches) also works
(`check_obs --world real`: 653 / 651 Hz per arm, 17/37 state dims finite).

**What is blocked:** (a) station cameras never reach a container on the
same host (R-24); (b) every station ROS node balloons to ~10 GB within a minute
of a camelo container participant joining the graph, twice today, ending in a
kernel OOM that took the desktop session, the tmux server and the ssh sessions
into the companion with it (R-22, R-23); (c) the runner (`run_policy`) has no
activation choreography, no keep-alive, and parks the arms at a **sim** pose on
start-up (R-27); (d) the controller's command input is a signed delta from the
pose captured at activation (R-14); the corpus actions are robot-frame
(§4 decision 2, measured offline), but whether the controller's sign vector
was in force when the corpus was recorded is undecided until the j1
direction probe T1b (§5) — no multi-joint command before it; (e) the companion clock drifts
~10 ms/min against the station and the controller rejects commands older than
0.5 s (R-16). Fixes for (c)/(d) and the bundle bugs of R-27 are landing in the
merge of `mb/non_gpu_inference` into this branch; (a)/(b) are the T2/T3 test
blocks of §5.

**Appendices:** [16a](16a_CORPUS_ACTION_FRAME.md) — the corpus action-frame
measurement (script + numbers); [16b](16b_CONTAINER_DDS_GUIDANCE.md) — the
container/DDS guidance report (ranked hypotheses, three set-ups, the T2
protocol), with one claim corrected inline.

### 0.1 Deploying the branch to the rig (rsync — no git on ebimHP)

ebimHP has no git credentials; the branch is deployed by **rsync from the Mac**,
to `~/camelo/camelo-ebim` on ebimHP (ext4 home, empty `~/camelo` already existed;
avoids the exFAT T7 drive's symlink/permission quirks). A `.deployed-commit`
marker travels with every sync so staleness is *detectable* — the T7 bundle went
stale silently precisely because nothing recorded what it was (R-25..R-29).

```bash
# on the Mac, from a CLEAN checkout of this branch (git status --short empty):
cd ~/workspace/camelo-ebim \
  && git rev-parse HEAD > .deployed-commit \
  && rsync -av --delete \
       --exclude .git --exclude outputs --exclude .venv \
       --exclude __pycache__ --exclude '*.pyc' --exclude .claude \
       ./ ebim@192.168.0.5:camelo/camelo-ebim/
# verify (must print the same SHA as `git rev-parse HEAD` on the Mac):
ssh ebim@192.168.0.5 'cat ~/camelo/camelo-ebim/.deployed-commit'
```

`--delete` keeps the target an exact mirror of the tree; the excluded dirs are
NOT deleted on the target (no `--delete-excluded`), so `outputs/` written on the
rig (probe CSVs/logs) survives re-syncs. Never rsync from a dirty tree — the
marker would claim a commit the files don't match. After a sync that touches
`docker/`, rebuild the image: `cd ~/camelo/camelo-ebim && ./scripts/ros_compose.sh build camelo-ros`.

Offline `make test` on the Mac has **2 known, pre-existing failures**
(`tests/test_train_slurm_num_workers.py`: BSD `date -Is` / bash 3.2 vs
`slurm/train.slurm`) — environment-only, green on Linux; do not chase them.
Baseline 2026-09-01: 821 passed / 14 skipped / these 2; `ruff` baseline 37.

### 0.4 T6 preparation, 2026-09-02 evening (after the compaction)

Everything here was done off the rig or read-only on it; **no arm was
activated and nothing on the rig was changed** during this pass. Read-only
rig state at 17:00 (R-62): clock **+0.42 s** (companion ahead — C-1 FAIL, re-sync
before any activation), arm stack up (`ctrl=4 faults=0 clients=2`; the 31
`grep -ci fault` hits are `DefaultDriverFactory` INFO lines, not faults — use
`grep -c '\[ERROR\]\|Rejecting GELLO'` instead), **no camera process running**,
deployed marker `bbdb316`, no tunnel to 8766, `~/pci-sim.pem` present on
ebimHP, `websockets` **missing** from the station pixi env (`msgpack` 1.2.2
present), `max_goal_velocity: 0.5` in both companion `controllers.yaml` copies,
wrist launches still at `enable_depth:=false` + the ineffective
`rgb_camera.color_profile` (U-21 open).

| Item | Status | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| Colleague's branch `mb/inference_server_ptI` | **MERGED** as `7c4df43` after a worktree trial merge + review: one `.PHONY` conflict, no change to `command_publisher`/`chunk_executor`/`episode_runner`, `make test` at baseline. Adds `--backend remote` client plumbing already present, `--dummy-obs` (a MOTION test: zero state + grey images → the policy commands a constant target at up to 1 rad/s per joint — never a wire smoke test), `scripts/run_dummy_client.py` (the real no-robot wire test), `make dummy-client`. Fix applied in the merge: `FixedObsBackend.adapter` passthrough so the adapter-vs-layout cross-check still runs under `--dummy-obs --backend local` | merge commit message; R-63 |
| Gripper unit chain for the remote path | **CORRECT as is** — the server's `--gripper-command` default `knuckle_rad` is inert: `LeRobotAdapter.infer` returns the raw 15-dim chunk (`coerce_chunk`), `rig_command()` (the only caller of `gripper_command`) has no callers; the client's `rig_chunk_to_canonical` copies dim 14 straight through, the executor clips 0..1 and `gripper_width_percent` publishes it as the 0..1 open fraction the driver takes (R-36). Same last two steps as the T5 replay that grasped (R-61). **No unit tag travels on the wire** (`pack_chunk`: `t0, dt, horizon, action_dim, actions, infer_s`) — if anyone ever routes the server through `rig_command`, the client cannot tell | review of `lerobot_generic.py:1387`, `s27a15.py:569`, `chunk_executor.py:141`, `command_publisher.py:459` |
| `--gello-joint-directions` in the colleague's command | ignored under `--arm-command-frame robot` (`command_publisher.py:265-267` returns the joints verbatim); harmless, equals the default | R-39/U-17 |
| U-23 stale-image guard | **CLOSED** — `7692eb5`: `--max-image-age-s` (default 0.5 s on `--world real`, off in sim, `0` = opt-out), checked at the replan/inference seam against the frame's wall receipt time, raises `StaleImageError` → the runner's guarded `finally` deactivates → `os._exit(1)`; `check_obs --world real` prints the same fault line; 15 tests | U-23 row |
| Parity gate 15 §1.2 (T6 entry criterion 4) | **A PASS — max abs difference exactly 0.0 on all 15 dims, every frame and chunk step of episode 9** on the EC2 box (A10G, `camelo-ebim/policy:latest`, PyTorch 2.10), the same checkpoint the server serves. **B FAIL by a hair**: gripper chunk vs the H100-stored probe max abs 1.199e-3 vs tol 1e-3 (mean 6.1e-5), per-dim MSE 1.7e-6 (tol 1e-4). A is the bar (15 §1.2); B's 1.2e-3 is the only non-zero number and is consistent with A10G-vs-H100 / torch-version float noise — **a hypothesis, not measured**; the H100 run of the same B read 0.0 (13 §P-1). Negative control `camera_blank`: **PASS, has teeth (max abs 0.746)** | R-62; `outputs/rig/munich_2026-09-01/parity/` |
| Policy server | up on EC2 (`ssh alpasim-ec2-a-10`, container `11f6f8ab1c69`, `camelo-ebim/policy:latest`, `serve_policy.py --adapter lerobot --checkpoint …/act/checkpoint --action-layout s27a15 --state-layout s27a15 --port 8766`), `ws://0.0.0.0:8766`, ACT chunk 21 / `n_action_steps` 10 / `chunk_dt` 0.05 s (the 20 Hz is the adapter's s27a15 fallback because the dataset `meta/info.json` is not resolvable from the checkpoint dir — it IS the corpus rate), GPU 475 MiB / 23 GiB. **The served rung is ACT, not 15 §2.1's first rung (MolmoAct2)** — see the decision below | EC2 read-only check 17:00 |
| U-24 start pose | **DATA IN (R-63): episode 9's frame 0 is an outlier** — 2nd-farthest of the 22 held-out episodes from the corpus frame-0 mean (max abs 0.73 rad, on j7 both arms); only 1 of 217 episodes lies within 0.10 rad of it on all 14 joints. The nearest held-out episode to the mean is **163** (max abs 0.163 rad), a genuine robot demo on video (R-56-style check PASS: Franka forearm branding visible, grasp frames 151–435 of 520, pad displaced at the end), red pad at x_norm ≈ 0.48 (= a protocol slot), frame-0 gripper 1.0. Artifacts built exactly like episode 9's: `outputs/rig/t5/{start_pose_s27a15_ep163.json, t5_ep163_home_pose.yaml, ep163_actions.npy, ep163_meta.json, ep163_frame0_head.png, ep163_contact.png}` (`rig_chunk_to_canonical` reproduces `ep009_actions.npy` to 0.0). Coverage fact for the tolerance: no single pose covers the corpus at 0.10 rad (0 of 217 within 0.10 of the mean; 2 within 0.20) — the start distribution's spread is 0.03–0.44 rad per joint, so `--start-pose-tol 0.10` is a *repeatability* check against the chosen file, not a corpus-membership check | R-63; `outputs/rig/t5/u24_frame0_stats.json` |
| Second review of the merge + U-23 guard (Opus, read-only) | **GO with one fix, applied**: `RemoteBackend._request` had no receive timeout (`camelo/policy/backend.py`), so a server that is alive on the socket but wedged in inference would block the 20 Hz loop forever — keep-alive holds the arm (safe half), but `--seconds` never expires and U-23's guard (sampled *before* `infer`) can never fire (bad half). Now `recv(timeout=15 s)` → `TimeoutError` escapes like `StaleImageError` → guarded deactivation. Guard itself: **no finding** (wall receipt time recorded per key per drained frame under the collector lock; decode failures leave it un-updated; no false fire at start — `wait_for_obs` precedes the first inference; `--cameras ''` is a verified no-op); deactivation on the exception path identical to the SIGINT path (keep-alive on the spin thread across the switch). Residuals noted: a camera republishing an identical frame at rate passes the guard (out of U-23's scope); `--dummy-obs` on real disables both camera guards (warning says so only partly; not used for T6); with `--backend remote` the layout cross-check is inert, so `--action-layout s27a15 --state-layout s27a15` must be on the command line (they are in §3.2 T6-6); `tests/test_contract_drift.py` skips without an `ebim-benchmark` checkout (contracts untouched by both commits) | review 17:35 |
| Slot sequence 15 §2.3 | **FROZEN** (numpy `default_rng(0)`): `outputs/rig/munich_2026-09-01/t6_slot_sequence.csv` (15 rows, 5 per slot at x_norm 0.48/0.51/0.61 = 614/653/781 px); `rollouts.csv` created with 15 §2.5's 37-column header, no rows | files |

**Addendum, end of 2026-09-02 (after R-64…R-66).** Landed after the 17:29
incident, all reviewed (Opus, GO) and on the branch at `f648c1e`, deployed to
ebimHP at 18:1x, **unmeasured on the rig** (R-66): U-27 `--max-state-age-s`
(0.2 s on real, every tick + pre-activation), U-28 (`--keepalive-hz` now a
dedicated thread, `--min-activation-publish-hz` 8 gate before the switch,
remote `reset` + one warm-up `infer` BEFORE activation, real arm-command
publisher depth 1, `activation_to_first_cmd_s` + `keepalive_max_interval_s`
telemetry), U-29 `--async-inference` (on for remote+real: the loop keeps
ticking at 20 Hz through the 0.45 s round trip, holds the last target on a
starved chunk). Reviewer's residuals to keep in mind: the cadence gate counts
keep-alive samples too, so "cadence OK" does not prove the hold loop was
healthy; the U-27 guard measures OUR callback receipt time, so on a starved
executor it can fire on a live rig (rerun with `--max-state-age-s 0.5`, never
`0`); the 1.076 s stamp age of R-64 has no measured explanation beyond clock
skew + queue depth, so **re-verify the clock offset immediately before every
activation**. Next session's first T6 run = §3.2 T6-1…T6-6 in order with T6-3
directly before T6-6; expected new log lines: `warmup_infer_s` ≈ 1.4 s before
activation, `activation publish cadence OK`, `policy request` / `policy chunk
… arrival_idx≈9-10/21` pairs every ≈0.45 s, ≈600 ticks in 30 s.

**Decisions proposed for the operator (not taken here):**

1. **First T6 rollout = ACT, right arm only, from episode 163's frame 0**, as
   the *pipeline* validation (deterministic, parity A = 0.0, the served rung).
   15 §2.1's scoring order (MolmoAct2 first) applies to *scored* blocks; MolmoAct2
   is not served and needs the seeded stochastic protocol. Record the first
   rollouts in `runs.csv` (block `T6.1`), not in `rollouts.csv`, until the three
   entry criteria below hold.
2. **U-26** (`max_goal_velocity` 0.5 → 1.0–1.2 rad/s, both companion copies +
   stack restart) and **U-21** (wrist colour profile) are both site-config
   changes. **U-21 CLOSED 2026-09-03 11:35 on the rig (R-73)** — not via
   `enable_depth:=true` (tried, ineffective) but via
   `config_file:=configs/d405_color_640x480.yml` on both wrist launches;
   `check_obs` confirmation still pending. U-26 remains open: without it every
   wrist error is a controller ceiling (R-55/U-26 — episode 163 itself peaks at
   1.42 rad/s on right j6). Before U-21's fix, running with 848×480 wrists
   meant the policy saw a shape it was never trained on and `run_policy`
   refused unless `--allow-camera-shape-mismatch`. **Do not score under U-26.**
   A T6.1 pipeline run under `--allow-camera-shape-mismatch` remains admissible
   only to prove the loop closes, e.g. if U-21's fix has not yet been confirmed
   by `check_obs`.
3. Home **both** arms to the episode's frame 0 (the policy's state is both
   arms; the left is held frozen by the publisher under `--arms right`), and
   re-run the C-15 overlay after homing (the left arm must be out of frame).

### 1.3 The arm controller contract (`joint_impedance_controller`)

Source: `~/ros2_ws/src/franka_fr3_arm_controllers/src/joint_impedance_controller.cpp`
and `config/controllers.yaml` on the companion.

| # | Fact | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| R-12 | Command topic is the relative `gello/joint_states` → **`/left/gello/joint_states`**, **`/right/gello/joint_states`**, `sensor_msgs/JointState`, subscription depth 1. **Names must be exactly `left_fr3v2_joint1..7` / `right_fr3v2_joint1..7` in order, 7 names and 7 positions**; anything else is rejected per message (`Rejecting GELLO state: joint name mismatch at index 0: got 'fr3_joint1', expected 'right_fr3v2_joint1'`, throttled 2 s) and activation then fails with `switch_controller ok=False`. Measured 17:44–17:45 with the unpatched bundle; fixed 17:47 → activation 17:50:57 succeeded. | `validateGelloJointState_` (cpp:368–392); `~/start_upper.log:650-674`; `activate_arms.py` output |
| R-13 | Stamp gates, by the **companion's** clock: reject if `header.stamp` is more than `future_timestamp_tolerance = 10 s` in the future, or older than **`max_gello_message_age = 0.5 s`**. Liveness: `hasFreshGelloState_()` = an *accepted* message within **`max_gello_liveness_gap = 2.0 s`** (local receive time). It gates `on_activate` (*"Refusing activation: no valid GELLO joint state received in the last 2.000 s"*) and, once the arm has reached its first goal, `update()` calls **`RCLCPP_FATAL` + `rclcpp::shutdown()`** — the `ros2_control_node` exits, `ros2 launch` treats it as required, the whole arm launch shuts down, `start_upper`'s trap follows (this is the 2026-08-29 `start_robot.log` death). **A stand-in's stream must never stop while the controller is active.** | cpp:70–90, `controllers.yaml:27-65` |
| R-14 | Mapping is a **delta captured at activation**: `on_activate` stores `q0 = measured` and `g0 = last accepted command`, then `q_goal = q0 + dir·(g − g0)` with **`gello_joint_directions = [−1, −1, +1, +1, +1, +1, −1]`**. Then a one-off motion-generator approach to the first goal (`motion_generator_speed_factor 0.05`), then tracking with the goal rate-limited to **`max_goal_velocity 0.5 rad/s`**. Gains `k = [240,240,240,240,100,60,20]`, `d = [20,20,20,10,10,…]` ~~full vector not read~~ — **corrected 2026-09-02 (U-26 analysis): `d_gains` full vector is `[20,20,20,10,10,10,5]`.** Consequences: a stand-in that publishes the *measured* pose before activation gives g0 = q0 (zero delta, no jump); joints 1, 2, 7 move **opposite** to a robot-frame command; low k on j6/j7 makes tracking compliant (R-30: ~60 % amplitude on j6, ~10 % on j7) — **narrowed 2026-09-02: R-55's wrist lag is the `max_goal_velocity` slew cap, not the gains, see U-26 (answered).** | `mapGelloToRobotGoal_` (cpp:326–336), `on_activate` (cpp:283–305), `controllers.yaml:86` |
| R-15 | The subscription is created at configure and **keeps receiving while inactive**, so the stand-in may (must) start before activation. `activate_arms.py` = one participant, discovers both `controller_manager` services first, then switches both; never two `ros2 control` calls (RUNBOOK S3). It printed `sequence size exceeds remaining buffer` once during discovery at 17:50:55 and still succeeded (§6). | cpp:97–105; `~/activate_arms.py` docstring; console 17:50 |

### 1.4 Clock

| # | Fact | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| R-16 | Companion − station skew, ssh-RTT compensated: **+0.216 / +0.214 / +0.216 s at ~17:03**, **+0.625 / +0.631 / +0.624 s at 17:46** → drift **≈ +10 ms/min** (companion ahead; `controllers.yaml` says it has no reachable NTP source and chronyd free-runs). Ahead-by-δ means every station-stamped command looks δ old on the companion; at δ > `max_gello_message_age` (0.5 s) **every command is rejected** and activation is refused as stale. | `date +%s.%N` triplets over `ssh companion`; `controllers.yaml:29-45` |
| R-17 | Fix = `cd ~/teleoperation/station && ./configs/sync_robot_clock.sh` (median of 7 samples over one multiplexed ssh, tolerance 0.05 s, ≤3 rounds, stops chronyd during the correction, applies a delta, re-measures). Internals (read 2026-09-02): tries `ssh -o BatchMode=yes "$HOST" "sudo -n /usr/local/sbin/tmr-set-clock $delta"` first; that helper and `/etc/sudoers.d/tmr-clock` are **absent on BOTH ebimHP and the companion** (`ls` → No such file on both), so it falls back to `ssh -t "$HOST" "sudo bash -c 'systemctl stop chrony …; date -s …'" </dev/tty` — an interactive sudo password prompt per round (≤3 rounds). **`--check` is safe to run any time**: measure-only, no sudo, one multiplexed ssh, 7×`date +%s.%N`, no DDS participant; it prints `(--check: no changes made)` and **exits 1 by design** whenever a correction is needed — that is not an error. The operator synced at ~17:48 and activation at 17:50:57 succeeded. **Sync before the robot is brought up** — a clock step under a live 1 kHz control loop was not tested (§6, U-6). | `~/teleoperation/station/configs/sync_robot_clock.sh`; `ls /usr/local/sbin/tmr-set-clock` (both boxes, 2026-09-02); `sync_robot_clock.sh --check` exit code |
| R-31 | **Companion clock reset to epoch 1970 on reboot** (2026-09-02, rebooted ~09:17 CEST, uptime 1:32 at ~10:50): `date -Is` = `1970-01-01T02:33:46+01:00`; `timedatectl` shows RTC also 1970, "System clock synchronized: no", chrony active with Reference ID `00000000`. `sync_robot_clock.sh --check` → `robot is 1788333453.977s behind the laptop NEEDS CORRECTION` (exit 1). Independent ssh-RTT-compensated triplet: **−1788333453.830 / −1788333453.834 / −1788333453.834 s** (companion BEHIND; RTT 307/300/301 ms). Per R-13 this is the *opposite* shape from R-16: station stamps are rejected as > `future_timestamp_tolerance` (10 s) in the **future**, not as stale; `on_activate` refuses either way. Attempts logged at 09:07/09:08/09:11 in `~/.ros/log` still had a correct 2026 clock — it was lost at the reboot itself; launch dirs from this boot are named `1970-01-01-01-14-13-…`. **Fix order:** arms DOWN (`pkill -INT -f start_upper.bash`) **before** `sync_robot_clock.sh` — a 56.7-year `date -s` step must never run under a live `ros2_control_node` (U-6) — then `--check` reads < 0.05 s, then bring-up. | `date -Is`, `timedatectl` over ssh; `sync_robot_clock.sh --check`; ssh-RTT triplet; `ls ~/.ros/log` |

| R-51 | **Wrist colour-profile mechanism found 2026-09-02, off-rig (`realsense-ros` tag `4.55.1` source + maintainer issues) — resolves U-21's parameter-name/value question; fix still UNTESTED on the rig.** (1) `profile_manager.cpp:453`'s regex `\s*([0-9]+)\s*[xX,]\s*([0-9]+)\s*[xX,]\s*([0-9]+)\s*` accepts `x`, `X` or `,` interchangeably — the comma-vs-x question (R-46) was a red herring; an unsupported combination logs `ROS_ERROR_STREAM("Given value, … is invalid. Run 'ros2 param describe …'")` and falls back to the previous/default profile, but only for a **declared** parameter. (2) Parameter groups are named after the physical librealsense sensor (`rs_node_setup.cpp:219`, `rs2_to_ros(sensor.get_info(RS2_CAMERA_INFO_NAME))`); the D405 has no RGB sensor — colour comes off the depth module — so a D405 node declares `depth_module.color_profile` and **never** `rgb_camera.*` (realsense-ros#3132: a live D405's full `ros2 param list` shows only `depth_module.*`; #3090, maintainer MartyG-RealSense: "the D405 … obtains RGB from the depth sensor, color uses the same resolution that depth is set to"). A launch override for an undeclared parameter is dropped by ROS 2 with no message — exactly R-46/R-49's silent no-op, on both the comma-form and x-form restarts alike, regardless of value syntax. (3) Stock `realsense2_camera/launch/rs_launch.py` at 4.55.1 lists `rgb_camera.color_profile`, `depth_module.depth_profile`, `depth_module.infra_profile` in `configurable_parameters` but **not** `depth_module.color_profile` — so the site's *original* launcher line (`depth_module.color_profile`, the right parameter name for a D405) was never forwarded by `rs_launch.py` to begin with (#3090, second finding). (4) The confirmation line for a profile actually taking effect is `ros_sensor.cpp:281`: `Open profile: stream_type: Color(0), Format: RGB8, Width: 640, Height: 480, FPS: 30` in the camera window — the 848×480 fallback prints the same line with `Width: 848`; that line, not another `check_obs` table, is what verifies the next fix attempt. (5) USB ruled out: a D405 enumerated as USB 2 is FPS-limited (1280×720 → 5 fps; 640×480 → 30 fps is fine), and R-49 Run B already has the wrists at 30 fps while still at 848×480, so the link is not the constraint. **Net: R-46's parameter-name switch to `rgb_camera.color_profile` fixed the wrong half of the bug — the D405 never declares that group — while the site's original `depth_module.color_profile` name was correct but structurally unreachable through `rs_launch.py`; neither restart tried so far could have worked.** Candidate fixes, smallest first, **all untested**: (a) `enable_depth:=true` on both wrist launches so colour follows the already-passed `depth_module.depth_profile:='640,480,30'` (costs a depth stream); (b) bypass `rs_launch.py` with a custom launch, or `ros2 run realsense2_camera realsense2_camera_node --ros-args -p depth_module.color_profile:=640,480,30 -p enable_color:=true`; (c) runtime `ros2 param set` + re-enable colour — forbidden while the FCI is up (C-9). | `realsense-ros` tag `4.55.1`: `profile_manager.cpp:453`, `rs_node_setup.cpp:219`, `ros_sensor.cpp:281`; issues realsenseai/realsense-ros#3132, #3090 (MartyG-RealSense); `realsense2_camera/launch/rs_launch.py`'s `configurable_parameters` list; researched 2026-09-02, not yet re-run on the rig |
| R-50 | **`check_obs` interpreter-exit hang — root cause found, fixed in `b565fc3`.** After printing every table, `check_obs` sat 6+ min in interpreter exit; Ctrl+C traceback: `threading._shutdown → concurrent.futures.thread._python_exit → t.join()`. Chain: `CameraWorkers.close()` SIGTERMed a worker mid-frame, leaving a truncated length-prefixed message in the `mp.Queue` pipe; the parent's drain callback was inside `Queue.get_nowait()`, which — after `_poll()` sees a partial read — falls through to a **blocking** `_recv_bytes()`; the parent still held the pipe's own write fd open, so EOF never reached the read end; `rclpy`'s `MultiThreadedExecutor` pool threads are non-daemon, so `_python_exit` joined the wedged thread forever — the process never exited even though it had already returned 0. **Fix:** close the parent's own writer before terminating workers (a blocked read then hits EOF instead of hanging), `poll()` becomes a no-op once closing has started, a bounded `shutdown_executor()` in `camelo/ros/session.py` that names any still-alive thread on timeout, and `os._exit` in `check_obs` matching the other real-world entry points. | `camelo/ros/session.py` (`shutdown_executor`), `camelo/ros/camera_workers.py` (`CameraWorkers.close`); Ctrl+C traceback, 2026-09-02 |

### 1.6 The two failures that ended sessions

| # | Fact | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| R-22 | **Global kernel OOM on ebimHP at 16:20:27 and 17:20:14–18.** In both, *every* station pixi ROS node had grown to **~9–10 GB RSS + 4–5 GB swapped, 50–75 GB virtual**: 16:20 — `realsense2_camera` 9.64 / 8.93 GB, `gello_publisher` 9.48, `labs_pedal_bridge` 9.46, `mode_manager` 9.29, `mobile_base_state_bridge` 9.15, `pedal_state_publisher` 8.96, `spine_state_publisher` 8.94; 17:20 — `mode_manager` 10.36 GB (51.6 GB vm, 2.2 GB swap), `spine_bridge` 10.7 GB anon / 60.9 GB vm at kill; Mem-Info 17:20:14 = 46.6 GB active + 50.0 GB inactive anon. Not one node's logic (`spine_bridge.py` was read: bounded futures, no growth path) — something acting on **all FastDDS participants of the station at once**, and *fast* (≥1 GB/s aggregate). | `journalctl -k --since 17:20:05 --until 17:20:40` (process table, `$(NF-7)` = rss pages) |
| R-23 | **Trigger correlation** (mechanism UNMEASURED): the nodes sat at ~0 % memory at 16:14 and the teleop ran 16:22→16:51 and 16:51→17:16 without growth; both onsets follow a **foreign participant joining the station graph on the same host** — ~20 `ros2` CLI participants 16:15–16:19, and `check_obs` (1 + 3 camera-worker participants) at 17:19:14 → 96 GB used by 17:20:14. Collateral at 17:20:18: kernel killed the two `ssh` sessions into the companion (→ SIGHUP → `start_upper` trap → arms down at 17:20:16–17), `spine_bridge`, `gnome-shell`, a `claude`, `chrome`; the desktop session `:0` ended and the tmux server started under it died with all panes. **Rule until T2 clears it: no camelo container participant on ebimHP while any station node runs; run every stack from a tmux started in an SSH login, never from the desktop.** | `last`, `who`, `journalctl -u ssh`; R-22 |
| R-24 | **Same-host discovery gap**: the Jazzy `camelo-ebim/ros:latest` container (host network, `ipc: host`, profile mounted, `FASTDDS_BUILTIN_TRANSPORTS=UDPv4`) discovers the **companion's** participants (arms at 650 Hz) but **none of the station's native nodes** — `check_obs --world real`: 0 frames on all three cameras, "no publishers on … yet", `/spine/joint_states` and odom silent. The 15:33 bag, recorded from the **Humble** `gello-humble` container, also contains only robot-side topics — so it is container ↔ native on one host, not a Jazzy-only effect. A native Humble/pixi participant did see everything at 16:16. Leads: the missing `127.0.0.1` whitelist entry (R-04); Humble-native ↔ container FastDDS on one interface. | `outputs/rig/check_obs_171914.log` in the bundle; bag metadata |

| R-35 | Two data-loss mechanisms found chasing R-32..R-34's missing CSVs (rows were rebuilt from the log in all four runs, `*_from_log.csv`): **(i)** Ctrl+C through `\| tee` breaks stdout → `BrokenPipe` inside the probe's interrupt handler → CSV/verdict skipped (T1(a)). **(ii)** rclpy's default SIGINT handler (`SignalHandlerOptions.ALL`) calls `rcl_shutdown()` **before** the probe's `finally`: the `ros-spin` thread dies with *"the given context is not valid"*, `publish_arms` raises *"publisher's context is invalid"*, `safe_stop` and the CSV are skipped, `make` exits 1 (T1(b), both T1(c) runs; also at t = 188.6 in `t1c_112332`, 1.4 s before the natural end). | **Consequence: a Ctrl+C could never deactivate a controller via service call.** Fix in progress on the branch: `rclpy.init(signal_handler_options=NO)` in `camelo/ros/session.py`, CSV + `<csv>.summary.txt` written first in the teardown, independent teardown steps, `--activate-arms` for the probe using the runner's `ArmControllers`. Until deployed: `\| tee -i` and let the probe end on its own timer (PROBE_SECONDS) — no Ctrl+C. |
| R-36 | **Gripper unit mismatch root-caused** — read-only recon on the companion, 2026-09-02, no ros2 CLI. `target_gripper_width_percent` (`Float32`) is wired as a **0..1 open fraction**, not 0..100 percent: `robotiq_gripper_client.py:27-35` dedups **in wire units** (`if abs(target - self.last_width) < 0.02: return`) then sends `goal_msg.command.position = 1 - gripper_position` (knuckle radians, 0 = open / **0.7929 = closed**, `robotiq_description/urdf/2f_85.ros2_control.xacro:18`; register = `pos/0.7929*227+3` clamped to [0,255], `ros2_robotiq_gripper/robotiq_driver/src/hardware_interface.cpp:276,297-299`, no clamp in `gripper_action_controller`). The station's own `gello_publisher.py:20,51,75-78` publishes the same "percentage (0-1)" fraction at 30 Hz; the corpus column ranges 0.2025..1.0. R-34's publisher sent 100.0/70.0/30.0 → `1 − 100 = −99 rad` → register clamps to 0 → **OPEN at every point of the sine** — the measured 1.00 was a *true* reading, not a stuck sensor. Every 20 Hz tick moved the wire value by ≈ 0.3, far over the 0.02 dedup threshold, so every tick issued a fresh `GripperCommand` goal that **preempted** the previous one (`gripper_action_controller_impl.hpp:28-39,93`, `setCanceled` with a default `Result`) — the 8011 `position=0.0, reached_goal=False` lines in `~/start_upper.log` are preempt results, not motion measurements. With correct 0..1 units the driver's own dedup throttles a 20 Hz stream to ≈ 3 goals/s per side with **no publisher-side rate limiting needed** (mirrors the site's own publisher). | **R-34's "gripper NOT MOVING" — CAUSE FOUND.** Fix lands in `camelo/contracts.py` (`gripper_width_percent` → 0..1); `--gripper-command width_percent` then becomes numerically identical to `open_fraction`. **Confirmed live by R-37** (2026-09-02, `t1c_115741`, span 0.7): corr(cmd, measured) = 0.991, `reached_goal=True` 651, non-zero positions. Note: the ≈900-goals/≈6×/s-per-side estimate below assumed span 0.3 and undercounts at other spans — **goal rate ≈ span·2/period/0.02 per side** (R-37 observed +1782/150 s at span 0.7, matching this formula, not a dedup failure); the `grip cmd = 0.70` ≈ 0.68–0.72 figure also assumed a ~1:1 command↔state scale that R-37 disproved — read expected values through `docs/CONTRACTS.md`'s map instead. Original (span-0.3) acceptance text, superseded: rows with `grip cmd = 0.70` read L/R ≈ 0.68–0.72 within ~1 s; companion `grep -c "Received & accepted new action goal"` delta ≈ 900 for a 150 s run (not ≈ 6000); `grep -o "position=[-0-9.e]*" ~/start_upper.log \| sort \| uniq -c` shows non-zero positions and `reached_goal=True` > 0. |

Our side: start pose OK (max 0.0003 rad), `controller_manager services up`, **"activation reference stationary … over 4 publishes in the last 0.60s"** (yesterday's replay: 11), `controllers ACTIVE on ['right'] after 1.8s` at 17:29:24.176, then `backend.reset` over the tunnel and **first inference 1.443 s** (chunk #1 at 17:29:25.752, 1.6 s after activation), later inferences 0.33–0.53 s (median 0.45 s) — every one blocking the 20 Hz loop: **90 ticks in 30.3 s, 58 chunks, clamped 57/90, max_requested_delta 0.187 rad/tick, keepalive_republished 159, max_publish_gap_s 0.206** (the pre-rollout gap is not in that figure). Companion (`~/start_upper.log`, companion clock): `Captured GELLO/robot reference poses` at 1788362964.213 (= activation), `[WARN] right.joint_impedance_controller: Rejecting GELLO state: message too old (timestamp age 1.075738 s, limit 0.500 s)` at +0.486 s, `[FATAL] Timeout: No valid joint states received from Gello` at +0.487 s → `Shutdown request received` → right `ros2_control_node` exits cleanly → launch: `process[ros2_control_node-8] was required: shutting down launched system` → left node, both grippers, state publishers all down (`joint_state_publisher-9` died with `publisher's context is invalid`, exit 1) → "An upper-body launch process exited (status 1); stopping the rest." **The arm was released at +0.49 s and never received a policy command.** Our runner did not notice: `/right/joint_states` stopped at that instant, `ObsCollector` served the frozen last state for the remaining 30 s (the joint-state twin of U-23 — U-27), the policy was fed the frozen state + live (static) images, `--joint-csv` "measured" is stale from row 1, and only the final `switch_controller (deactivate)` timed out (BEST_EFFORT 5 s, STRICT 5 s). Operator: "Looks like the gripper died on both" — it was the whole stack. Clock at 17:3x: companion +0.25 s. **Verdict: T6-6 FAIL by mechanism = command-stream freshness in the post-activation window (controller demands a < 0.5 s-old command within 0.5 s of activation); policy behaviour UNMEASURED.** Three suspects, all with the same fix direction: (i) publish cadence collapsed before activation (4/0.6 s) and the runner activated anyway; (ii) the remote path's reset + 1.44 s first inference after activation left only the keep-alive to keep the stream fresh, and the keep-alive (a ROS timer on the same executor as the camera drain) ran at ≈5 Hz; (iii) the 1.076 s stamp age itself is not explained by the 0.25 s clock skew — an unexplained residual. R-65 separates (i)/(ii) from the camera load. Fixes landing: U-27 (joint-state liveness guard), U-28 (keep-alive on its own thread, activation cadence gate, remote warm-up BEFORE the switch, post-activation gap accounting), U-29 (asynchronous inference — 0.45 s RTT vs 0.4 s replan interval starved the loop to 3 Hz). |

Evidence gathered afterwards (read-only, EC2 + ebimHP): (a) at 18:2x the tunnel listener on ebimHP was up (`127.0.0.1:8766`, ssh pid 2598629) and a raw TCP connect through it succeeded locally but **no byte came back within 5 s**; (b) on EC2 the ORIGINAL server container `11f6f8ab1c69` was gone and a new one, **`clever_ellis`, started 16:02:09 UTC = 18:02:09 CEST** by the colleague with the identical `serve_policy.py` command, was listening on `0.0.0.0:8766` — my `docker ps | grep serve_policy` missed it because `{{.Command}}` truncates to `python3 -u scripts/…`, so my "container gone" reading at 18:20 was WRONG and my detached restart attempt failed with `address already in use` (harmless, removed); (c) **`clever_ellis`'s log shows NO client connection between 18:02 CEST and 21:38 CEST** (when a local handshake from the EC2 host itself completed in 0.00 s and was logged) — neither the 18:14 attempt nor the operator's second attempt (18:2x, `exit=1`, log unread — the station left the network) ever reached the server. **Verdict: the tunnel, not the server or the client** — the `ssh -N -L` session had been idle since 17:30, carries no `ServerAliveInterval`, and a NAT/idle drop leaves the local listener accepting connections that go nowhere (exactly (a)). | R-86 | **Gripper latch a04/a05, 15:44–15:54 CEST — a threshold-only latch cannot separate the pre-shape dip from the close; a proximity gate (df5bf68) is untested on the rig.** `--gripper-latch CLOSE_BELOW[:HOLD_S[:RELEASE_ABOVE]]` (`ec62d7f`): engage once the commanded fraction drops below `CLOSE_BELOW`, hold ≥ `HOLD_S` at 0.20, release only after 1 s above `RELEASE_ABOVE`. **a04** = `t6a_a04_154452` (15:44, `--gripper-latch 0.4:30:0.9`; gripper had NOT been re-opened at start — measured 0.01 at t=0, the policy opened it to ≈0.6): latch ENGAGED at tick 194 (9.7 s) on a pre-shape dip to 0.399 while the arm was still 0.22 rad from the grasp pose — closed beside the pad (operator: "closes too early"); the policy then went into its transport motion (1.55 rad away by 20 s); operator Ctrl+C at 22 s. **a05** = `t6a_a05_155215` (15:52, `--gripper-latch 0.3:30:0.9`, gripper re-opened first): grasp pose reached at 13 s (L2 0.13) but the policy never closed (command min 0.71), the arm wandered off (L2 1.0 by 24 s), operator Ctrl+C at 30 s — the latch never engaged. **Conclusion: with the offset splice the approach is reliable** (a02–a05 all within 0.1–0.2 rad of the demo's grasp pose by 10–20 s) **; the close is the checkpoint's coin flip** (the handoff probe reads the gripper channel as ≈ a state copier), and when it does come, a ≈3 s ramp; a threshold-only latch cannot tell the pre-shape dip (0.4–0.6 rad for several seconds) from the real close. `df5bf68` adds `--gripper-latch-near slot:x048:0.25` (engage only within 0.25 rad of the slot's demo grasp pose) to gate on proximity as well as threshold — **untested on the rig** (a06 planned). Score files beside each CSV (`tools/rig_probes/score_t5_trace.py`). |
| R-87 | **Gripper latch at 0.3, three runs 16:11–16:31 (deployed df5bf68; the R-83 offset line, ACT on 8767).** a06 `t6a_a06_161140` (`--gripper-latch 0.3:25:0.9`, 53 s): latch ENGAGED at 36 s (command 0.292) with the arm 0.32 rad from the demo grasp pose, measured gripper 0.01 from then on, the policy then ran its transport motion (1.3 rad away by 44 s). a06 `t6a_a06_161952` (same flags, 36 s, started near the grasp pose): ENGAGED at 8 s at 0.20 rad, held closed, hovered within 0.3–0.5 rad for the rest. a07 `t6a_a07_163120` (`--gripper-latch 0.3:20:0.9`, 34 s): ENGAGED at 21 s at 0.27 rad (right j1..j7 [1.02, −0.60, −1.66, −1.94, −1.86, 2.65, −0.59] vs demo f200 [1.10, −0.63, −1.72, −1.91, −2.09, 2.60, −0.56]; j5 the only joint off by > 0.1), closed at 24 s at 0.18 rad, held until the operator stopped it. With 0.3 the latch no longer fires on the pre-shape dip (a04) and did fire in all three runs at the grasp pose, unlike a05 where the policy never reached 0.3. **Physical outcome (pad in the fingers / lifted) not reported by the operator before the wind-down** — the traces say "closed at the demo grasp pose", nothing more. Mechanism PASS ×3. | The latch + offset splice is the day-end default line (16c); `--gripper-latch-near slot:x048:0.25` (df5bf68) remains untested on the rig. |

The CSV of R-30b was lost: Ctrl+C reached `tee` before the summary and the
script's post-loop block was skipped on `KeyboardInterrupt` — fixed the same
evening (the probe now writes CSV + verdict on Ctrl+C and exits 130).

## 2. Pre-execution checklist — every session, every run

Tick every row; a "no" is a stop, not a note.

| # | Check | How | Pass |
|---|---|---|---|
| C-1 | **Clock** (R-16/17/31) | `cd ~/teleoperation/station && ./configs/sync_robot_clock.sh` **before** bringing the robot up; `--check` any time. Redo if > 20 min have passed since the last sync or the robot was rebooted. **After ANY companion reboot expect the clock at 1970** (R-31) — the sync is then a step of *decades*, not milliseconds: arms down first, never under a live control loop (U-6). | `robot is 0.0xx s … OK` (< 0.05 s) |
| C-2 | **Terminal hygiene** (R-23) | Every long-running stack in a tmux started from an SSH login shell (`tmux new -s rig`), never from the desktop; companion stacks detached (R-09). Check parentage with `ps -o pid,ppid,args -p <pid>` up the chain; a desktop parent (e.g. `terminator`/`gnome-shell` on `:0`) = restart it in the R-09 form. | `tmux ls` shows the session; `who` shows the desktop is not its parent |
| C-3 | **No station nodes while a camelo container participant exists** (R-22/23), until T2 clears it | `ps -eo pid,args \| awk '/pedal_state_pub[l]isher\|spine_brid[g]e\|realsense2_camera_n[o]de\|zed_open_captur[e]\|gello_pub[l]isher/'` prints nothing before `ros_compose.sh run …` | empty |
| C-4 | **GELLO publishers off** whenever a stand-in publishes on `/left\|right/gello/joint_states` (depth-1 topic: two publishers = jitter between two targets) | same `awk` as C-3 on `gello_pub[l]isher` | empty |
| C-5 | **Arms-only bring-up** (R-08/09) | detached `start_upper.bash`; wait for the gripper "Configured and activated" lines | exactly 4 `ros2_control_node` |
| C-6 | **Memory baseline** (R-22) | `free -g` on ebimHP; during any container run poll `ps -eo rss,args --sort=-rss \| head -5` every ~10 s | used < 5 GB and flat |
| C-7 | **Fault lines** (R-10) | First `ls -l --time-style=+%F_%T ~/start_upper.log` and compare its mtime with the stack's start — a stack started without R-09's `> ~/start_upper.log` redirect leaves the log stale (2026-09-02: mtime 2026-09-01 18:30, `grep -c` = 4 = yesterday's shutdown lines, while the live stack had 0 fault lines and 0 `Rejecting GELLO` in its own launch dir). If stale, grep the newest `~/.ros/log/*/launch.log` instead (`ls -t ~/.ros/log/*/launch.log \| head -2`), and prefer restarting in the R-09 form so the log stays live. Otherwise: `grep -c -iE 'FATAL\|reflex\|communication_constraints\|died' ~/start_upper.log` | 0 |
| C-8 | **Safety** | E-stop in a hand from activation on; arm workspace clear; nothing in the grippers if the gripper is cycled; every other Claude/agent session on the station paused (no `ros2` CLI, no containers) | operator says so |
| C-9 | **No `ros2` CLI on either box while the FCI is up** (R-25) | — | — |
| C-10 | **Code on the station = the rsync-deployed branch tree `~/camelo/camelo-ebim`** (§0.1; ebimHP has no git), never the T7 copy `/media/ebim/T7/camelo/camelo-ebim-mb-non_gpu_inference` (stale, hand-patched, `.bak` files) | `cat ~/camelo/camelo-ebim/.deployed-commit` vs `git rev-parse HEAD` on the Mac; `ls camelo/**/*.bak*` empty | SHA matches and ≥ `f467d4d`, no `.bak` |
| C-11 | **Real-path imports present in the env that runs the probe/runner** (R-38) — `controller_manager_msgs` was missing in BOTH the station pixi env and the container on 2026-09-02, and it is only needed for `--activate-arms`/`--wait-for-activation`. **Updated 2026-09-02: present in the station pixi env since 12:35 (`pixi add ros-humble-controller-manager-msgs`, R-38's fix, confirmed by R-41's run) and in both images** (Jazzy `camelo-ebim/ros:latest`, rebuilt 12:17, and Humble `camelo-ebim/ros-humble:latest`, built 12:39) — passing this check in the Jazzy container is still necessary but not sufficient for activation (see C-12). | `python3 -c "from camelo.ros.real_imports import report_real_imports; print(report_real_imports())"` in whichever env/interpreter will run the probe/runner | no `MISSING` line |
| C-12 | **The participant that switches controllers is Humble-generation** (`rmw_fastrtps_cpp` 6.x / Fast DDS 2.6.x) — R-40: a Jazzy container's `controller_manager` service calls discover the service but never get an answer (`switch_controller`/`list_controllers` time out), even with `controller_manager_msgs` installed (C-11). Use set-up C (native pixi) or set-up B (Humble image) for any run that activates/deactivates controllers | check the interpreter's `rclpy`/`rmw_fastrtps_cpp` package generation matches the companion's (Humble, apt) — station pixi and set-up B both qualify, the Jazzy container does not | Humble-generation, not Jazzy |
| C-13 | **T1b still valid** — the joint-1 direction verdict (R-39) contradicts the installed `gello_joint_directions` (U-17); re-run T1b (`--joints 1 --amplitude 0.02`) after any rebuild/relaunch change of the companion's `franka_fr3_arm_controllers` before any multi-joint command | `ls -l ~/ros2_ws/install/franka_fr3_arm_controllers/lib/*.so` mtime unchanged since the last T1b, else re-run | mtime unchanged, or a fresh IN-PHASE verdict |
| C-14 | **Native runs use camelo's DDS profile, not the site's** (R-48/R-49) — the site's rendered Fast DDS profile has no socket buffer sizes, and a 208 KB default `net.core.rmem_default` drops most of every fragmented camera sample under our participant | `make dds-profile PY=python` + `export FASTRTPS_DEFAULT_PROFILES_FILE=$PWD/outputs/rig/fastdds_camelo.xml` **AFTER** sourcing `tmr_laptop_env.sh` | verify with `nstat -az UdpRcvbufErrors` delta ≈ 0 across a `check_obs` run |
| C-15 | **Head-camera view matches the corpus frame-0 view** (R-58/U-25) — the mobile base's position/orientation relative to the table, not just the arms' joint configuration, must match the recording before any rollout is scored | capture a live head-camera frame at the PTP-homed task-2 pose and overlay it (side-by-side and 50/50 blend, R-58's method, §3.1 E4) against `outputs/rig/t5/ep009_frame0_head.png`. **Iterate — push the base, re-capture, re-overlay** (R-58 → R-59 → R-60 took two iterations; by eye alone the base was ≈1 m off). Also home the arms so neither is in frame, as in corpus frame 0 | table far edge ≈ 20 % from the top of frame (R-60 met it at ≈22 %), near edge at the bottom, pads mid-frame, fixture at the right — matching corpus frame 0. Record any scene difference (R-60: red pad in slot 3 vs episode 9's slot 4) with the rollout. **R-77 finding: the mean-pixel-difference overlay gate cannot see a shifted/reordered pad row** — R-76/R-77's scene had the live pad row at 75 px spacing, RED in slot 3, while still scoring a "matching" overlay, because the whole-frame diff averages over it. **Recommended addition to this gate: a per-pad blob-centroid check** — red pad centroid at x ≈ 615 ± 10 px, teal centroids at x ≈ 697 / 772 / 839 px (episode 163 frame 0, 97 px spacing) — run alongside the mean-pixel-difference overlay, not instead of it |
| C-16 | **`websockets` importable in the station pixi env** (the remote client imports it lazily at `camelo/policy/backend.py:48`; `msgpack` is already there). `pixi add websockets` — a site env change like C-11's | `pixi run python -c "import websockets, msgpack"` | T6 |

"Healthy" reads as: 4 controller managers ~28 %/~5 % CPU, `free -g` flat,
`~/start_upper.log` growing only with gripper client chatter, and (once the
runner's keep-alive lands) no `Rejecting GELLO state` lines in the log.

**C-3 narrowed (2026-09-02, R-43):** the ban is CONTAINER-specific — a
native pixi participant coexisted with the station camera nodes for 24 min
with flat RSS (T2 steps 0–2). Only a **camelo container** participant is
banned pending T2 steps 3–4.

## 3. The validated sine-probe procedure (as run 17:39–17:55)

Three terminals **on ebimHP**, inside an SSH-started tmux. Commands verbatim.
A 300 s probe gives a clock: activate at t ≈ 5–20 s, deactivate at t ≈ 90 s,
**never later than t ≈ 200 s**, Ctrl+C only after deactivation.

**Step 1 — Terminal A, companion upper body** (C-1 done first):
```bash
ssh companion
TMR_WS=~/ros2_ws setsid nohup bash ~/start_upper.bash --restart > ~/start_upper.log 2>&1 < /dev/null &
tail -f ~/start_upper.log       # Ctrl+C stops the tail only
ps -eo pid,etime,args | awk '/ros2_control_n[o]de/' | cut -c1-90   # want 4 lines
```
Gate: 4 controller managers, gripper spawners "Configured and activated", 0
fault lines. If `--restart` prints the SIGTERM/143 pair, that is the old
instance (R-09).

**Step 2 — Terminal B, the probe (nothing moves yet):**

Two forms exist. **native (set-up C)** — **validated end-to-end 2026-09-02
(R-32..R-34: 3 runs, arms at ~1100 Hz, 0 faults)**; set-up C has positive
on-box evidence now — keep the container form below as the fallback:
```bash
cd ~/camelo/camelo-ebim   # the rsync-deployed branch tree (§0.1), NOT the stale T7 copy (C-10)
source ~/teleoperation/station/configs/tmr_laptop_env.sh
make sine-probe-real PY=~/teleoperation/station/.pixi/envs/default/bin/python \
  PROBE_SECONDS=300 PROBE_ARGS="--arms right --no-gripper --hold-s 40"
```
The Makefile's `PY ?= python3` resolves to `/usr/bin/python3` (3.12.3, numpy
1.26.4, **no `rclpy`** → `ModuleNotFoundError` at `camelo/ros/command_publisher.py:48`)
— it does not run bare. The station's RoboStack pixi interpreter above (3.11.15;
rclpy/sensor_msgs/std_msgs/geometry_msgs/numpy/yaml/msgpack/PIL present,
`websockets` missing and irrelevant to the probe) imports `CommandPublisher`,
`ObsCollector`, `ros_session`, `wait_for_obs` cleanly against the deployed tree
and is the same FastDDS 2.6.10 binary the station teleop nodes use, no
container participant — 16b's set-up C.

**container (what ran 2026-09-01)** — verbatim, and still the only form with
positive on-box evidence today:
```bash
cd ~/camelo/camelo-ebim   # the rsync-deployed branch tree (§0.1), NOT the stale T7 copy (C-10)
CAMELO_ROS_GPU=0 FASTRTPS_DEFAULT_PROFILES_FILE=/tmp/tmr_fastdds_laptop_1000.xml \
EBIM_BENCHMARK_ROOT=/media/ebim/T7/camelo/benchmark \
./scripts/ros_compose.sh run --rm -T --name camelo-sine-probe camelo-ros \
  python3 -u scripts/sine_probe_real.py --seconds 300 --amplitude 0.03 --arms right --no-gripper \
  2>&1 | tee outputs/rig/sine_probe_$(date +%H%M%S).log
```
Expected within ~10 s: `start pose left/right=[…]` (all finite; compare with
R-20 by eye), `wiggling ['right'] +/-0.03 rad …`, then one row per second:
`meas R(j6,j7)=(+0.0000,+0.0000)`, `js_hz` climbing to ~700. Gate: rows
streaming, **no** `Rejecting GELLO state` lines in Terminal A for 10 s
(they mean names/stamps are wrong — stop, do not activate). `no complete
observation within 30s` = arms not publishing → back to step 1.

**Step 3 — Terminal C:** `ssh companion` (login only, keep open).

**Step 4 — activate, at `t = 5…20`:**
```bash
timeout 60 python3 ~/activate_arms.py
```
Expected: `all services discovered; activating now`, then
`left: joint_impedance_controller activated`, `right: … activated`. Then a slow
approach (≤ 0.03 rad, speed factor 0.05) and `meas R(j6,j7)` tracking `cmd`.
`switch_controller returned ok=False` = the controller refused (stale stream
R-13/R-16 or names R-12): Ctrl+C the probe (safe, still inactive), read
Terminal A, fix, restart from step 2. Arm not moving 30 s after activation, or
motion on the wrong arm/joints → step 5 immediately.

**Step 5 — deactivate at `t ≈ 90`, then stop the probe, in this order:**
```bash
python3 ~/activate_arms.py --deactivate     # wait for both "deactivated"
# Terminal B: Ctrl+C  → start pose republished, safe_stop, CSV + verdict printed
```
Never Ctrl+C or let the probe time out while the arms are active (R-13). **With
the branch as of 2026-09-02 morning, do NOT Ctrl+C at all: use `PROBE_SECONDS`
so the probe ends by itself** (hold 40 + wiggle 150 = 190 s), deactivate at
t ≈ 130 (R-35 — Ctrl+C loses the CSV and, worse, cannot reach the deactivate
service call).

**Step 6 — wind down (optional):** `pkill -INT -f start_upper.bash`.
Restoring teleop (`pixi run gello` etc.) only with no camelo container running
(C-3).

**Read-only gates between steps** (an agent can do these over ssh without a
DDS footprint): companion `ps` count, `~/start_upper.log` grep, ebimHP `free -g`
and `docker ps`, the probe log tail. Abort conditions: any `FATAL`/`reflex`
line, a controller manager disappearing, any station node > 2 GB RSS, station
`used` memory rising by > 1 GB/min.

**Trap, found 2026-09-02 (R-50, fixed in `b565fc3`):** a ROS entry point
(`check_obs`, the probe, the runner) that **returned 0 but does not exit** is
not a hang without a cause — it is a wedged non-daemon executor thread stuck
in a blocking read. Do not `kill -9` and move on: read the `shutdown_executor`
warning for the thread name first, it names exactly which thread is stuck.

### 3.1 Validated native procedure (set-up C), 2026-09-02

Everything below **ran on the rig on 2026-09-02** and is quoted from the run
that produced the row named beside it. Anything not run today is marked
**UNVERIFIED** in place. Same three-terminal discipline as §3 (Terminal A =
companion `start_upper.bash`, Terminal C = a second `ssh companion` login);
§3's step 1, step 3 and step 6 are unchanged and are not repeated here.

**A — the environment, once per shell (C-14, R-49 Run B).** One command per
block; do not collapse them, the order matters (`tmr_laptop_env.sh` exports the
site profile and would overwrite the camelo one).

```bash
cd ~/teleoperation/station && pixi shell
```
```bash
source configs/tmr_laptop_env.sh
```
```bash
cd ~/camelo/camelo-ebim && make dds-profile PY=python
```
```bash
export FASTRTPS_DEFAULT_PROFILES_FILE=$PWD/outputs/rig/fastdds_camelo.xml
```
Expected: `make dds-profile` prints its own diff against
`/tmp/tmr_fastdds_laptop_1000.xml` — **exactly two inserted lines**,
`receiveBufferSize` 16 MiB and `sendBufferSize` 4 MiB on transport descriptor
`eth_udp`, nothing else changed (R-49).
Gate (pure `print`, zero DDS):
```bash
python -c "import sys, os; print(sys.executable); print('PROFILE=', os.environ.get('FASTRTPS_DEFAULT_PROFILES_FILE'), 'DOMAIN=', os.environ.get('ROS_DOMAIN_ID'), 'RMW=', os.environ.get('RMW_IMPLEMENTATION'))"
```
`sys.executable` must be
`/home/ebim/teleoperation/station/.pixi/envs/default/bin/python` (3.11.x),
`PROFILE=` the camelo file, `DOMAIN=0`, `RMW=rmw_fastrtps_cpp`. Anything else
and this is not set-up C — stop. (`CameraWorkers` spawns workers by
re-executing `sys.executable` and inheriting `os.environ`, so the profile and
domain reach them with no extra plumbing — `camelo/ros/camera_workers.py:160`.)

**B — the probe, self-activating (R-41, `t1r_123718`).** No `activate_arms.py`:
the probe drives the runner's own `ArmControllers`, activating **inside** the
hold so `g0 = q0` (R-33/U-4).

```bash
python -u scripts/sine_probe_real.py --arms right --no-gripper --hold-s 40 --seconds 150 --activate-arms > outputs/rig/t1r_$(date +%H%M%S).log 2>&1; echo "exit=$?"
```
Expected, in order: `controller_manager services up: ['/right/controller_manager/list_controllers', '/right/controller_manager/switch_controller']`
→ `switch_controller activate ok (BEST_EFFORT) on ['right']` → `ACTIVATED at
t=1.01s, offset(t_act)=+0.0000` → one row per second, `js_hz` climbing to
~1100. Gate: hold-phase `max |meas|` = 0.0000, **no** `Rejecting GELLO state`
in Terminal A for 10 s, 4 controller managers. A `did not answer within 5.0s`
here means the participant is not Humble-generation (C-12, R-40) — stop.
**Ctrl+C is now safe and deactivates first** (SIGINT deferred across the whole
teardown, `020a0c2`): `switch_controller deactivate ok` → `DEACTIVATED at
t=…, list_controllers confirmed=True`, then CSV + summary. **One Ctrl+C only** —
a second one during "settle" cost R-41 its summary file.

**C — cameras, with the UDP-drop wrapper (R-49 Run B, `t3_checkobs_144847`).**
`nstat -az` is read-only and takes no DDS participant.

```bash
nstat -az UdpRcvbufErrors
```
```bash
python -u scripts/check_obs.py --world real --seconds 10 > outputs/rig/t3_checkobs_$(date +%H%M%S).log 2>&1; echo "exit=$?"
```
```bash
nstat -az UdpRcvbufErrors
```
Expected table (R-49 Run B, all three cameras): `recv` **30.0/s** = the wire
rate, `decoded` 25/s (the pre-decode limit), `shipped` 20/s, ingest
`image_head 19.8 image_wrist_left 19.7 image_wrist_right 19.8` Hz, frames
198/197/198, `gap` min 0.026/0.033/0.033 s, `dropped_full` 12–15 per 3 s report
(the 25 Hz decode outpacing the 20 Hz drain — expected, not loss), shapes head
`(720, 1280, 3)` **on contract**, wrists `(480, 848, 3)` **off contract**.
Gates: `recv` ≈ wire rate on every camera; `UdpRcvbufErrors` delta a few hundred
at most (R-49: +781, host-wide, cost no image sample — vs +2,938 without the
camelo profile). **`exit=3` was expected while the wrists read 848×480 — it is
the wrist shape-drift alarm, not a rate or discovery failure; a healthy run
without the alarm would still exit 1 by construction (U-18).** U-21 CLOSED
2026-09-03 (R-73, `configs/d405_color_640x480.yml`): a run since then should
show wrist shape `(480, 640, 3)` and exit 1, not 3 — `check_obs` confirmation
is still pending. Read the tables, never the exit code.

**D — the RSS watcher (verbatim, as used for R-43/R-45's 301 samples).** Its
own tmux window; `PIDS` enumerated **once**, never in a `pgrep -f` loop.

```bash
TS=$(date +%Y%m%d_%H%M%S); ps -eo pid,args | awk '/gello_pub[l]isher|realsense2_camera_n[o]de|zed_open_captur[e]|pedal_state_pub[l]isher|mode_mana[g]er|spine_brid[g]e|spine_state_pub[l]isher|mobile_base_state_brid[g]e|labs_pedal_brid[g]e/' | tee ~/camelo/camelo-ebim/outputs/rig/t2_pids_$TS.txt; PIDS=$(awk '{print $1}' ~/camelo/camelo-ebim/outputs/rig/t2_pids_$TS.txt | paste -sd,); echo "$PIDS"
```
```bash
while :; do { date +%T; ps -o pid,rss,etime,cmd -p "$PIDS"; CO=$(ps -eo pid,args | awk '/check_ob[s]\.py/ {print $1; exit}'); [ -n "$CO" ] && ps -o pid,rss,etime,cmd -p "$CO" $(pgrep -P "$CO" | tr '\n' ' '); free -m | awk '/^Mem:/{print "MEM used="$3" free="$4}'; echo; } >> ~/camelo/camelo-ebim/outputs/rig/t2_rss_$TS.log; sleep 5; done
```
The `check_ob[s]\.py` awk is self-excluding; `pgrep -P` matches by parent PID
(no self-match hazard) and catches the spawned camera workers, whose cmdline is
`multiprocessing.spawn`. Gate: every watched PID flat (±50 MB), `MEM used`
flat. Abort at **+1 GB on any single PID**.

**E — the T5 sequence (R-52 → R-53 → R-55 → R-59).** In this order, E-stop in
hand from the homing step on. All four ran today.

E1 — dry run, dummy adapter (R-52). `--no-start-pose-check` is admissible
**only** here, because the dummy adapter commands the measured pose (zero delta
by construction); never with a trajectory.
```bash
TS=$(date +%H%M%S); python -u scripts/run_policy.py --world real --backend local --adapter dummy --arms left,right --no-start-pose-check --activate-arms --keepalive-hz 10 --rate 20 --max-delta 0.05 --cameras '' --seconds 30 --joint-csv outputs/rig/t5dry_$TS.csv > outputs/rig/t5dry_$TS.log 2>&1; echo "exit=$?"
```
Gate: `q0 == g0` on both arms, `activation_q0_g0_drift_rad 0.0`,
`clamped_pct 0.00 %`, `max_publish_gap_s` ≈ 0.06 (fault threshold 2.0),
`keepalive_republished 0`, self-deactivation confirmed, worst joint error
~1e-5 rad — the arms must not move.

E2 — home to the episode's frame 0, on the **companion** (R-53). The largest
motion of the day (PTP 0.15 rad/s, up to 3.3 rad): workspace check + E-stop
first.
```bash
ssh companion
```
```bash
python3 ~/home_arms.py --file ~/t5_ep009_home_pose.yaml
```
Add `--side right` for one arm only. The yaml carries episode 9's frame-0
measured joints in `teleop_home_pose.yaml`'s schema — **not**
`teleop_home_pose.yaml` itself (R-47). Gate: `left: at home.` **and**
`right: at home.` (or `already at home … skipping.`), `PTP motion completed
successfully` in `~/start_upper.log`, 0 faults, 4 controller managers.

E3 — the replay, right arm (R-55, `t5_replay_ep9_155241`). The left arm is
held: frozen at its measured pose by the publisher, and since `39fa124` it is
no longer start-pose-verified.
```bash
TS=$(date +%H%M%S); python -u scripts/run_policy.py --world real --backend local --adapter replay --checkpoint outputs/rig/t5/ep009_actions.npy --arms right --start-pose file:outputs/rig/t5/start_pose_s27a15_ep009.json --start-pose-tol 0.10 --activate-arms --wait-for-activation --keepalive-hz 10 --arm-command-frame robot --rate 20 --max-delta 0.05 --replan-steps 8 --seconds 22 --joint-csv outputs/rig/t5_replay_ep9_$TS.csv > outputs/rig/t5_replay_ep9_$TS.log 2>&1; echo "exit=$?"
```
Gate: `right: start pose OK` (all joints ≤ 0.0002 rad in R-55), left reported
`held, not verified`, `dt=0.050` on the first chunk line (`dt=0.033` = the
`*_meta.json` was not found — **abort**, the episode is about to play 1.5×
fast), `clamped_pct` ≈ 6–10 % (21 of episode 9's 341 frames exceed the clamp by
construction, R-47), 0 stale chunks, self-deactivation confirmed. Never widen
`--start-pose-tol`; a `StartPoseNotReached` refusal is the check working
(R-54). **Two-arm replay: UNVERIFIED** — never run (U-25).

E4 — the scene check (R-58/R-59, C-15). `capture_head.py` **must be a file on
disk**, never a heredoc piped to `python -`: `CameraWorkers` spawns its worker
by re-importing the parent module by path, and a program read from stdin has no
path to re-import.
```bash
python -u outputs/rig/capture_head.py > outputs/rig/live_head_$(date +%H%M%S).log 2>&1; echo "exit=$?"
```
It opens an `ObsCollector` with `--cameras head` only, waits for one complete
observation and writes `outputs/rig/t5/live_head_<HHMMSS>.png`. Then, offline
(Mac/tiger3, no rig participant), overlay it against
`outputs/rig/t5/ep009_frame0_head.png` as a side-by-side **and** a 50/50 blend
(R-58's method) — C-15's gate: table far edge ≈ 20 % from the top of frame,
near edge at the bottom, pads centred. **Iterate**: push the base, re-capture, re-overlay — R-58 (≈1 m off, placed by
eye) → R-59 (≈25 %) → R-60 (≈22 %, C-15 met) took two iterations. The overlay
is the base-placement instrument, not a post-hoc check. Also home the left arm
to its corpus rest pose so it leaves the frame.

**UNVERIFIED in this section** (drafted but not run on 2026-09-02, do not treat
as validated): the container forms of any of the above (T2 steps 3–4, U-20);
`--cameras head --allow-camera-shape-mismatch` as the T5 camera fallback;
`enable_depth:=true` on the wrist launches (U-21's candidate fix); the two-arm
episode-9 replay (U-25); any `--backend remote` invocation (T6).

### 3.2 T6 procedure — DRAFTED 2026-09-02, UNVERIFIED on the rig

Native pixi (set-up C) throughout; **no container**, because the participant
that switches controllers must be Humble-generation (C-12) and the colleague's
`ros_compose.sh run … camelo-ros` form is the Jazzy container that failed R-40.
Same terminal discipline as §3/§3.1; one new window **Terminal T** (the tunnel).
Blocks in order; every gate must hold before the next block.

**T6-0 — deploy + site env (Mac, then ebimHP).** §0.1's rsync from a clean tree
(expected marker = `git rev-parse HEAD` on the Mac). `outputs/` is excluded
from the rsync, so the episode-163 artifacts go separately:
```bash
scp outputs/rig/t5/start_pose_s27a15_ep163.json outputs/rig/t5/t5_ep163_home_pose.yaml outputs/rig/t5/ep163_actions.npy outputs/rig/t5/ep163_meta.json outputs/rig/t5/ep163_frame0_head.png ebim@192.168.0.5:camelo/camelo-ebim/outputs/rig/t5/
```
```bash
ssh ebim@192.168.0.5 'scp ~/camelo/camelo-ebim/outputs/rig/t5/t5_ep163_home_pose.yaml companion:~/ && cat ~/camelo/camelo-ebim/.deployed-commit'
```
The remote client needs `websockets` in the station env (site change, like
`controller_manager_msgs` on 2026-09-02):
```bash
cd ~/teleoperation/station && pixi add websockets
```
Gate: `pixi run python -c "import websockets, msgpack; print(websockets.__version__)"` prints a version.

**T6-1 — Phase 0 as §0.2** (clock with the arms DOWN, detached bring-up, stale
gripper clients killed, cameras up, RSS watcher), then §3.1 A (env, DDS
profile, the `print` gate). Clock gate: `< 0.05 s`.

**T6-2 — tunnel (Terminal T on ebimHP, plain shell, no pixi needed).**
```bash
ssh -N -o ServerAliveInterval=15 -o ServerAliveCountMax=3 -o ExitOnForwardFailure=yes -L 8766:127.0.0.1:8766 -i ~/pci-sim.pem ubuntu@54.224.145.114
```
**The public IP changes when the instance is stopped and started** (35.171.188.196 on 2026-09-02, 54.224.145.114 on 2026-09-03) — take it from the EC2 console / `~/.ssh/config` on the Mac each morning, and the server container must be started again after every instance restart (`docker ps --format '{{.Names}} {{.Status}}'`, port 8766 free → the `docker run` from §0.4).
Gate, in another window: `ss -ltn | grep 8766` shows `127.0.0.1:8766` — **necessary, not sufficient** (U-30/R-66: a dead transport keeps the local listener). The sufficient gate is T6-3, run again immediately before every T6-6; if the tunnel window has printed anything or exited, restart it first.

**T6-3 — wire smoke test, NO robot participant — MANDATORY immediately before every T6-6 (U-30)** (pixi shell, §3.1 A done; the
dummy client neither subscribes nor publishes):
```bash
python -u scripts/run_dummy_client.py --server ws://127.0.0.1:8766 --rate 2 --seconds 20 2>&1 | tee outputs/rig/t6_dummy_$(date +%H%M%S).log
```
Gate: every request answered, action shape `(21, 15)`, `infer_s` well under
0.4 s (the replan interval at `--replan-steps 8` × 0.05 s); no reconnects. The
server window on EC2 logs one line per request (`docker logs -f 11f6f8ab1c69`).

**T6-4 — cameras at contract shape.** `check_obs` per §3.1 C. Gate: **no
`SHAPE MISMATCH` line**. **U-21 CLOSED 2026-09-03 (R-73)**: the wrists reach
640×480 via `config_file:=configs/d405_color_640x480.yml` on both wrist
launches, not `enable_depth:=true`; `check_obs` confirmation is still pending
— if it has not yet run, or the wrists still read `(480, 848, 3)`, T6-6 may
run with `--allow-camera-shape-mismatch` **for the pipeline check only**, and
nothing from it is scored.

**T6-5 — start pose.** E-stop in hand; largest motion of the block.
```bash
ssh companion
```
```bash
python3 ~/home_arms.py --file ~/t5_ep163_home_pose.yaml
```
Gate: `left: at home.` and `right: at home.`, `PTP motion completed
successfully`, 0 faults. Then the C-15 overlay (§3.1 E4, now
`python -u scripts/capture_head.py` from the deployed tree, overlay with
`tools/rig_probes/overlay_head.py <live> outputs/rig/t5/ep163_frame0_head.png <out>`
on the Mac): both arms out of frame, table/pads as in `ep163_frame0_head.png`
(red pad at x_norm ≈ 0.48). Place the red pad per `t6_slot_sequence.csv` row 0
(also 0.48).

**T6-6 — the rollout, ACT, right arm, 30 s** (pixi shell; camera window and
watcher up; video recording on; E-stop in hand). `--task` must be the frozen
caption byte-for-byte — the server refuses a drifted one.
```bash
TS=$(date +%H%M%S); python -u scripts/run_policy.py --world real --backend remote --server ws://127.0.0.1:8766 --action-layout s27a15 --state-layout s27a15 --task "Pick up the thermal pad and place it on the target RAM board" --arms right --start-pose file:outputs/rig/t5/start_pose_s27a15_ep163.json --start-pose-tol 0.10 --activate-arms --wait-for-activation --keepalive-hz 10 --arm-command-frame robot --rate 20 --replan-steps 8 --max-delta 0.05 --max-image-age-s 0.5 --seconds 30 --joint-csv outputs/rig/t6_act_$TS.csv > outputs/rig/t6_act_$TS.log 2>&1; echo "exit=$?"
```
Expected, in order: real-import preflight silent; `right: start pose OK`,
`left: held, not verified`; camera shapes on contract (or the refusal);
`controller_manager services up` → `switch_controller activate ok` →
`ACTIVATED`; chunk lines with `dt=0.050`, one inference every 8 ticks
(≈2.5 Hz), `infer_ms` a few tens; `clamped_pct` reported (no oracle value —
the policy is not the corpus); self-deactivation confirmed; joint CSV +
summary. **`StaleImageError` or a websocket error must end with
`DEACTIVATED … confirmed=True`** — that is the guard working. Add
`--allow-camera-shape-mismatch` only under T6-4's proviso. Score with
`tools/rig_probes/score_t5_trace.py <csv>` (no `.npy` — there is no recorded
reference for a policy rollout) and record one `runs.csv` row (block `T6.1`).

**Two-arm and longer rollouts** (`--arms left,right --seconds 60`): only after
T6-6 has closed the loop once, with the workspace clear of the left arm's swing
(15 §1.7). **Scored rollouts** (`rollouts.csv`, 15 §2.5, seed `0` for ACT):
only with U-26 closed (U-21 CLOSED 2026-09-03, R-73, pending `check_obs`
confirmation) and the slot sequence followed.

## 4. Decisions taken on 2026-09-01 (from the blocker list)

| # | Blocker | Decision | Lands as |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Runner parks arms at a sim pose (R-27) | **On real, recentre is verify-only + hold.** ~~The start pose is the site's GELLO/teleop home pose (`station/configs/teleop_home_pose.yaml`, what `home_arms.py` drives to on the robot side, = corpus frame 0 by construction).~~ **FALSE for the task-2 corpus, R-47:** zero of 217 episodes start within 0.10 rad of `teleop_home_pose.yaml` — the closest differs by 2.42 rad (max over joints), and the corpus occupies a different region of joint space entirely (both arms' j1 ≈ +1.5 rad vs the home pose's −1.07/+0.81). The start pose file must instead come from the corpus: episode frame 0 for replay (T5), or a pose inside the training start distribution for a policy rollout (T6) — e.g. the per-joint mean of frame 0 across all 217 episodes, or a specific held-out episode's frame 0 (R-47). The arms must be driven there by the companion's `home_arms.py --file <yaml>` (PTP at 0.15 rad/s, deactivates the impedance controllers itself, `AT_HOME_TOL 0.05`) **before** the runner verifies within `--start-pose-tol 0.10`. **Never** widen that tolerance, and never let the runner's own executor cover the 2+ rad gap from `teleop_home_pose.yaml` to a task-2 start pose at its 1.0 rad/s command rate — that gap is `home_arms.py`'s job, at its slower, position-controlled speed, not the impedance-controller executor's. The operator homes the arms with `home_arms.py` **before** activation; the runner then publishes the *measured* pose (zero delta, R-14), checks it against the expected start pose within a tolerance and **refuses** the rollout otherwise. No camelo code ever commands a pose through the impedance controller that it did not observe or that a policy did not emit. | merge: `--start-pose` default for `--world real`, verify-only `recenter_arms`, hold phase |
| 2 | Action frame of the s27a15 corpus (leader vs robot; `*_follower_gello_joint_states_*` columns vs R-14) | **Measured offline on tiger3 (2026-09-01 evening; `task2_munich_s27a15_hires`, 217 eps / 121,828 frames, and the 22 held-out eps): the recorded `action` arm columns are ROBOT-FRAME.** For all 14 arm joints the OLS slope of action on state is in [0.992, 1.018], Pearson r in [0.989, 0.9995], intercept ≤ 0.02 rad, residual std a few hundredths of a rad (largest on j7), and the correlation peaks at a 2–5 frame (100–250 ms) action lead — ordinary follower servo lag. No joint shows the −1 slope a raw leader-frame command would show on joints 1, 2, 7. Report + script: [16a](16a_CORPUS_ACTION_FRAME.md). **Consequences:** s27a15's "arms as absolute robot joint radians" is confirmed on the policy-output side. It does **not** settle the controller side: the stand-in publishes on the controller's *pre*-transform input (`/left\|right/gello/joint_states`) and the controller applies `q = q0 + dir·(g − g0)`, `dir = [−1,−1,1,1,1,1,−1]`, afterwards. Either the corpus column was recorded *post*-transform (then the publisher must apply `g = g0 + dir·(q_target − q0)` on joints 1, 2, 7) or the directions were effectively identity at recording time (then identity publish is right). Today's j7 data cannot decide it (k = 20 + friction: j7 sat at +0.025 rad and oscillated ±0.002). **Decision:** the runner gets `--arm-command-frame {gello,robot}` with the direction vector as a parameter mirroring `controllers.yaml`; the DEFAULT is decided by T1b, not by assumption. | merge: `--arm-command-frame`; default ← T1b |
| 3 | No `sine` adapter | Not needed: the sine test stays `scripts/sine_actor.py` / `sine_probe_real.py`, outside the runner. | — |
| 4 | Inference stalls the command stream (R-13/R-29) | All policies run **remote** (`--backend remote`). Runner gets an arm **keep-alive ≥ 10 Hz** republishing the last command from a timer, and a measured liveness budget: websocket RTT + inference must stay ≪ 2 s; log the max gap per rollout. | merge |
| 5 | Gripper `Float64` (R-18/28) | Fix to `Float32`; `--gripper-command width_percent`. | merge |
| 6 | Cameras never reach the container (R-24) | Report in [16b](16b_CONTAINER_DDS_GUIDANCE.md). **Ranked hypotheses, discovery gap:** (1) ~~container never loads the profile~~ — **ruled out for the on-site bundle as measured** (`/tmp:/tmp` mounted, `exists=True` inside the container) — but that measurement was against the on-site bundle's unpushed hotfix, not the merge: this branch's `docker/compose.yaml` itself lacked the `FASTRTPS_DEFAULT_PROFILES_FILE` passthrough and `/tmp:/tmp` mount until 2026-09-01 evening, now landed; (2) the lost `127.0.0.1` whitelist entry (R-04) — real, but native↔native works over the same file, so compounding not sufficient; (3) Fast DDS version skew — station `ros-humble-fastrtps` **2.6.10** (RoboStack, verified) vs Jazzy container **~2.14 (unverified)**, with real discovery-surface changes in between (XCDRv2 in 2.12, Type Look Up Service flow controller in 2.13); (4) dual-NIC + `network_mode: host` (rmw_fastrtps#786); (5) GUID/PID collision — fixed upstream 2020, `pid: host` is free hardening; (6) SHM — both sides already UDPv4-only; (7) discovery range — default SUBNET; (8) QoS mismatch — wrong symptom shape (would list an incompatible publisher, not zero). **OOM:** (1, unifying) version-skew retransmission storm on the builtin SEDP endpoints every node runs — fits "every node at once" but is reasoned, not found in a primary source; (2) an uncapped RELIABLE writer history on a station node fed by a slow-to-ACK reader; camelo-side leaks cannot inflate native RSS. **Three set-ups:** **A** fix the `sed` so `127.0.0.1` survives (`/127\.0\.0\.1/!s#…#…#g`) + `pid: host`, keep Jazzy; **B** a Humble-based remote-backend-only image (`ros:humble-ros-base`, no torch/lerobot); **C** run the ROS side natively in the station's pixi env (same 2.6.10 binary the teleop nodes use; the only option with positive on-box evidence today). **Order: C, then A's fixes regardless, B in reserve — for anything that only needs topics.** **Promoted 2026-09-02 (R-40): B is now REQUIRED, not reserve, for anything that calls `controller_manager` services from a container** — R-40 measured service request/reply broken across the Jazzy↔Humble gap (discovery succeeds, `switch_controller`/`list_controllers` never answer) while topics cross the same gap cleanly; C (native pixi, Humble-generation) is the other viable participant for services, blocked only by `controller_manager_msgs` (R-38). The rebuilt Jazzy image (12:17, 2026-09-02) now includes `controller_manager_msgs` — **R-38 corrected**: the import gap that first blocked `--activate-arms` is fixed, but R-40 shows a container is still the wrong participant for services regardless, because the underlying issue is DDS version skew, not the missing package. | 16b §2; T2/T3 |
| 7 | OOM trigger (R-22/23) | Scheduled as a controlled experiment, T2. | §5 |
| 8 | Clock drift (R-16) | A per-execution check, C-1. | §2 |
| 9 | Real-robot code lives only in `mb/non_gpu_inference`; s27a15 + parity tool only here | Branches merged into this branch. | merge |

Runner features landing with the merge (so the next probe is the runner, not a
script): hold-until-activated (publish measured pose from the first complete
observation), activate/deactivate both arms from the **runner's own participant**
(same service choreography as `activate_arms.py`), deactivate-on-exit including
on exceptions, keep-alive ≥ 10 Hz, `--arm-command-frame` with the R-14 transform
`g = g0 + dir·(q_target − q0)`, start-pose verification with tolerance and
refusal, `Float32` grippers, wrist shape 480×640.
