## 5. Test plan for the coming days

Ordered; each block has entry criteria (all must hold), exit criteria (the
measurement that ends it) and the rows to record. A block that fails its exit
criterion is diagnosed by mechanism (15 §1.2's failure-shape table, R-12…R-17)
before anything downstream is attempted. Every run, every block:
**one row** in `outputs/rig/munich_2026-09-01/runs.csv` —
`ts_iso,block,run_id,branch_sha,who,skew_s_before,upper_bringup_ts,station_nodes(list),container_participants,activation_ts,deactivation_ts,max_meas_offset_rad_json,faults,mem_used_gb_max,verdict,verdict_reason,log_path,notes`.

**T1 — sine probe on the merged branch (regression + extension).** Entry: C-1…C-10; branch tree rsync-deployed to ebimHP per §0.1 (`.deployed-commit` ≥ `f467d4d`); `make test` green offline. Runs, in order: (a) right arm only, `--no-gripper` — must reproduce R-30b (max |meas| j6 ≥ 0.015, left 0.0000, 0 faults); (b) `--arms left,right`; (c) grippers on (`--gripper-span 0.3`, nothing in the grippers) — record the knuckle-rad swing from `/…/gripper/joint_states` and which way `1.0` moves (first data point of 12 §2 item 3). Exit: three FOLLOWED verdicts with per-second rows and CSVs, and the same procedure driven by the **runner's** hold/activate/deactivate path instead of `activate_arms.py`. **Note (2026-09-02):** `scripts/sine_probe_real.py` does **not** use the runner's `RealArmSession`/`ArmControllers` (`camelo/runner/real_arms.py`, `camelo/ros/arm_activation.py`) — it shares only `CommandPublisher`/`ObsCollector` — so the last exit criterion needs either a probe flag that reuses `ArmControllers` (planned 2026-09-02, not yet written) or a `run_policy.py --world real --backend local --adapter dummy --activate-arms` hold-only run (exists today; holds, does not wiggle). Runner flags that already exist for this: `--activate-arms`, `--wait-for-activation`, `--deactivate-on-exit` (default on), `--keepalive-hz` (10), `--arm-command-frame` (default `robot`, decided by T1b), `--start-pose file:<path>` + `--start-pose-tol` (0.10 rad); sim poses are refused unconditionally on real. **Status (2026-09-02):** (a)
**DONE — FOLLOWED** (R-32); (b) **DONE — FOLLOWED** (R-33); (c) **arm DONE /
gripper DONE — FOLLOWED** (R-34/R-36/R-37 — wire units 0..100 vs the
driver's 0..1, fix confirmed live 2026-09-02); the runner-path run (last
exit criterion above) is **DONE** (R-41, native pixi form) — **T1 is fully
complete.** Both routes to a Humble-generation participant now exist: (i)
native pixi, after `pixi add ros-humble-controller-manager-msgs` landed the
missing package in the station env (R-38's fix, R-41's run); (ii) the Humble
image `camelo-ebim/ros-humble:latest`, built 12:39 on ebimHP from
`docker/Dockerfile.ros-humble` (commit `fd2b6e4`) — an import smoke test
through the entrypoint passed (`import rclpy, controller_manager_msgs.srv`
OK), but set-up B is only **BUILT**: its service path and camera discovery
against the companion/station are **UNMEASURED** — see U-20.
**Status 2026-09-02 end: COMPLETE** — (a) R-32, (b) R-33, (c) R-34/R-36/R-37,
runner path R-41; all natively, 0 faults.

**T1b — direction probe on joint 1 (before any multi-joint command).** Entry: T1(a) FOLLOWED; C-1…C-10. A `sine_probe_real.py` variant/flag that wiggles **only joint 1** of the right arm (dir = −1, k = 240, base rotation) at **±0.02 rad**, same choreography as §3 (activate at t ≈ 5, deactivate ≤ t ≈ 90). The probe must **hold cmd = start pose (zero offset) for the first 10 s** so activation always captures g0 = q0 — today's activation at t ≈ 19.5 s captured a non-zero offset and the tracked wiggle sat on a constant ≈ −offset(t_act) (j6 tracked in [0.008, 0.045] for a ±0.03 cmd) — and print offset(t_act). Record the per-second rows. **Verdict:** measured j1 **in phase** with cmd → identity publish is correct → `--arm-command-frame robot` becomes the default; measured j1 **anti-phase** → the transform `g = g0 + dir·(q − q0)` is required → default `gello`. **Abort rule:** any |meas j1| > 0.05 rad → deactivate immediately. Exit: one unambiguous phase verdict with rows, and the default written into the runner and into `docs/CONTRACTS.md` with the date. **Status (2026-09-02): DONE — IN PHASE** (R-39, `t1bj1_120702`, corr = +0.922, max \|meas\| 0.0173/0.02 rad, 0 faults). Identity publish confirmed; `--arm-command-frame robot` is the default, measured not assumed. `docs/CONTRACTS.md` updated with the date (below). **Status 2026-09-02 end: DONE** (R-39) — re-run after any companion rebuild (C-13); the `dir = −1` contradiction stays open as U-17.

**T2 — OOM / same-host discovery experiment** (R-22…R-24; protocol from [16b §3](16b_CONTAINER_DDS_GUIDANCE.md), 30–45 min). Entry: T1 done; **arms not activated** (FCI may be up but nothing commanded — a discovery burst then costs nothing); no `ros2` CLI on either box for the whole block; the station `used` memory baseline < 5 GB. Constraints throughout: one participant added at a time; **tear the just-added participant down between steps and let RSS return to baseline** before the next; **abort at +1 GB RSS on any single watched station PID** — kill the newcomer immediately, log which PIDs moved and at what elapsed time, let RSS settle, then decide; the container's own PID goes into the same watch. Set-ups A, B, C are tested **one at a time, never stacked**.

| Step | Add | Watch | Expect / abort rule |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0 (5 min) | nothing — start station `pixi run cameras` only (**not** `pixi run pedal` — protocol deviation, recorded 2026-09-02: T3 needs the camera publishers up, and `pedal` brings up four more of the nodes that ballooned in R-22); enumerate the station PIDs **once** (`ps -eo pid,args \| awk '/gello_pub[l]isher\|realsense2_camera_n[o]de\|pedal_state_pub[l]isher\|mode_mana[g]er\|spine_brid[g]e\|spine_state_pub[l]isher\|mobile_base_state_brid[g]e\|labs_pedal_brid[g]e/'` — never a `pgrep -f` loop, it self-matches); background watcher appending `ps -o pid,rss,etime,cmd -p <pids>` every 5 s to `outputs/rig/t2_rss_<ts>.log` | baseline RSS per PID | flat |
| 1 (5 min) | nothing more | same | flat — any growth here means the trigger is not the container: stop and re-scope |
| 2 (5–10 min) | **one native Humble participant** from the station pixi env: a scripted, time-bounded rclpy subscriber to one camera topic (hard timeout; not an open-ended `ros2 topic echo`) | same, 5 min | flat. +1 GB here means same-version participants are unsafe too and version skew drops out of the ranking |
| 3 (10–15 min) | **one bare container participant**: `camelo-ros` running only `rclpy.init()` + an empty node + spin — deliberately not `check_obs`, to separate "a participant joined" from "a participant subscribed" | from t = 0, ≥ 3 min (prior onset ≈ 60 s) | growth here = builtin/discovery-traffic hypothesis (16b problem-2 #1) → **the key data point**; abort, tear down, confirm RSS recovers |
| 4 (10–15 min, only if 3 was clean) | container + camera subscriptions: `check_obs --world real --seconds 10` (the real `camera_workers` path); read its `report_publishers()` lines — a 0 → > 0 transition answers the discovery question with no CLI. **Its exit code is not the gate here either** (U-18) — a healthy run still exits 1 (T3, below); read the printed lines, not the shell status. | same | growth here but not in 3 = user-topic reliable-writer/history problem (16b problem-2 #2) |
| 5 | repeat 3–4 under each candidate set-up in turn: **C** (native pixi participant instead of the container — i.e. step 2's environment running `check_obs`), **A** (sed fix so the profile has one `172.16.16.118` and one `127.0.0.1`, verified by `grep -A3 interfaceWhiteList /tmp/tmr_fastdds_laptop_1000.xml`, + `pid: host`), then **B** only if C is impractical | same | — |

**Note (2026-09-02) on step 2's shape:** `check_obs --world real --seconds 10 --cameras head` **is** step 2's "one native Humble participant … time-bounded rclpy subscriber to one camera topic" — 2 participants (the `check_obs` node + 1 spawned camera worker). The all-three-camera form, `check_obs --world real --seconds 10`, is 4 participants — the same shape as the run that preceded the 17:20 OOM (R-23). Run the single-camera form (2a) before the all-three form (2b), never the reverse.

Rows to record per step: set-up, participant added, per-PID RSS series (file), max RSS delta, onset time if any, discovered-topics list from `report_publishers()`. Exit: one configuration in which every station node stays < 500 MB for 10 min with the participant present **and** the participant discovers `/spine/joint_states` and the three camera topics. Until then C-3 stands.

**Status (2026-09-02): steps 0–2 PASS in set-up C** (native pixi; R-43) — RSS flat through the single-camera participant (step 2, `check_obs --cameras head`) and for a 19-min soak after, and (R-45) the all-three-camera participant. **Steps 3–4 (bare/camera CONTAINER participant) not run** — per R-40/U-20 the container form is only meaningful once tested against a Humble-generation image; the Jazzy container is already known-broken on the services plane (R-40) and its camera-discovery behavior is separately unproven. Until steps 3–4 run, C-3 stands for CONTAINER participants (narrowed 2026-09-02, §2).
**Status 2026-09-02 end: steps 0–2 PASS in set-up C (R-43/R-45); steps 3–4 NOT RUN (container, U-20); T2's exit criterion is therefore met for native only.**

**T3 — cameras into the container.** Entry: T2 exit. `check_obs --world real --seconds 10`: **per-process ingest ≥ 18 Hz for each camera** — not "≥ 20 Hz", which is unreachable by construction: the camera drain timer is created at 0.05 s (`camelo/ros/obs_collector.py:118`), `_drain_cameras` → `CameraWorkers.poll()` drains each queue to a single latest frame (`camelo/ros/camera_workers.py:246-255`) and is counted once per camera per tick (`obs_collector.py:272`), and `rates()` = count/wall (`obs_collector.py:568-571`) — so neither the printed per-camera Hz nor the `sim Hz` column can ever exceed ~20 Hz (19.x with timer jitter). The drain-timer ceiling is 20 Hz; if the ceiling itself is the constraint for a policy, raise the timer, not the bar. **Column semantics, re-derived 2026-09-02 from R-45's rows:** `frames`/`sim Hz` count parent DRAIN-tick records (one per camera per `_drain_cameras` tick that finds a non-empty queue), so they read the drain ceiling, not the camera — MEASURED 18.2–18.4 Hz here, from the mean stamp gap of the head camera's records (0.0543–0.0550 s). `gap(sim-s)` is the MEDIAN stamp-to-stamp gap of *recorded* frames, not the wire period: under an ~18 Hz drain, a 30 fps wire aliases (more than half the gaps are two wire periods, so the median reads exactly 0.0667 s while the *mean* gap equals the drain period), but a wire slower than the drain has every frame recorded and the median IS the wire period — read `gap` against the wire rate, not the drain ceiling, when diagnosing a camera. Also record shapes 1280×720 / 640×480 / 640×480, and `image_age` sane. `ros2 topic hz` measures the wire, not ingest — AGENTS.md. Record the printed table verbatim. **The exit code of `check_obs --world real` is not the gate on this rig** (U-18): `_real_topics()` declares `expected_obs_streams=("clock","joint_states_full","odom")` (`camelo/contracts.py:336`), and `odom`'s base stage never comes up (R-07) → `scripts/check_obs.py:128-137` returns 1; independently `min_finite = 37-14 = 23` (`check_obs.py:116`) vs the 17 finite dims arms+grippers give → `check_obs.py:138-139` returns 1 — so a fully healthy T3 run still exits 1. Read the printed tables; do not gate on the exit code. **Acceptance also requires shape parity**: each camera's ingested shape must equal the corpus resolution the checkpoint was trained on — head 1280×720, wrists 640×480 (`docs/CONTRACTS.md`) — arriving frames are not enough; with the site's launcher as configured on 2026-09-02 the head reads 376×672 (VGA, R-44) and both wrists read 480×848 (RealSense default profile, launcher argument apparently ineffective, R-45), so **ingest can PASS while shape parity FAILs** — that is a real-vs-training distribution shift even when every rate number looks healthy. **Status (2026-09-02, R-45, `t3_checkobs_130618`, set-up C):** all three cameras DISCOVERED and INGESTED — discovery/ingest **PASS**, U-2 fully answered for native. **Acceptance FAILs** on both counts above, and re-reading the rows against the wire rate (not just the drain ceiling) collapses them into one launcher bug: `wrist_left` 14.6 Hz and `wrist_right` 8.4 Hz both trace to the wrists negotiating a 15 fps wire instead of 30 (a launcher argument-name bug — `depth_module.color_profile` isn't a color parameter of `realsense2_camera` 4.55.1), the same bug that put them at the wrong shape — **U-21 broadened to cover the rate finding too, merging what was tracked as U-22**; `wrist_right`'s further shortfall below even that 15 fps wire is a separate, narrower per-run contention loss, tracked as the now-narrowed **U-22**. **Status (2026-09-02, R-49, `t3_checkobs_144847`, set-up C, with `make dds-profile`'s 16 MiB/4 MiB socket buffers): INGEST PASS all three at 19.7–19.8 Hz with the camelo profile; SHAPE: head OK, wrists 848×480 pending U-21.** The launcher fix landed in three edits: a parameter-name fix (13:2x, comma-form value, still 848×480 — the value format was never the issue, R-51), a second attempt at the value format (14:25, x form, still 848×480 — R-49), and, separately, the receive-side buffer fix (R-48/C-14). `t3_checkobs_140915` first confirmed the head at the corpus resolution `(720, 1280, 3)` with a true 30 fps wire (min stamp gap 0.030 s, no aliasing); R-49 confirms it again in both its runs. On the rate side, R-49's Run A (site DDS profile, no socket buffers) reproduced R-48's mechanism directly (`UdpRcvbufErrors` +2,938 over 10 s, `recv` 11–21/s against a 30 fps wire); Run B (camelo's profile) drove `recv` to the full **30.0/s on all three cameras** and ingest to **19.8 / 19.7 / 19.8 Hz** — **U-22 is answered: the rate shortfall was UDP receive-socket-buffer overflow, fixed by C-14's 16 MiB buffers, not a per-run contention loss** (the "loser flips sides" symptom was the buffer race, not a separate mechanism). On the shape side the wrists are unmoved by either the x-form value or the buffer fix: `depth_module.color_profile: 640x480x30` was the site's original argument, `rgb_camera.color_profile: 640x480x30` reaches the node now (R-49, `/tmp/launch_params_*`) — and the D405 still streams 848×480 either way, so **U-21 narrows to whether the D405's colour stream is owned by `depth_module.color_profile` (the site's original NAME, paired with the x-form VALUE) or whether 640×480 is simply not a supported colour profile on this D405** — see U-21. **Mechanism found off-rig 2026-09-02 (R-51): the D405 has no RGB sensor and never declares `rgb_camera.*`, so R-46's switch to that name was itself wrong, while the site's original `depth_module.color_profile` name is right but is not in stock `rs_launch.py`'s forwarded-parameter list — neither restart tried could have worked.** Next step, untested: **wrist launcher: `enable_depth:=true` (colour follows depth on the D405), verify `Open profile … Width: 640` in the camera window, then check_obs with the camelo DDS profile.** The container form (T3's original scope) is still blocked on T2 steps 3–4 / U-20.
**Status 2026-09-02 end: INGEST PASS all three at 19.7–19.8 Hz with C-14's profile (R-49 Run B); SHAPE head PASS (720×1280), wrists FAIL (848×480) → `check_obs` exit 3; U-21 open with a diagnosed but untested fix (R-51).**

**T4 — gripper units and polarity, live** (12 §2 items 2–3, 15 §1.4). Entry: T1(c), and (2026-09-02) the `camelo/contracts.py` `gripper_width_percent` → 0..1 fix from R-36 deployed. (1) open by command → raw knuckle ≈ 0.0 rad, state[14] ≈ 1.0; (2) close on the thermal pad → raw ≈ 0.7929 rad (driver constant, R-36 — not yet observed live), state ≈ 0.01; (3) `open_fraction = 0.2` through the adapter with `--gripper-command width_percent` → it **closes**. Exit: the three numbers, frozen into `docs/CONTRACTS.md` with the date. **Status (2026-09-02, R-37):** item (3) **confirmed live** — the gripper follows the commanded open fraction (corr = 0.991 at lag 0), and the `measured ≈ (cmd−0.2)/0.8` map holds within ~0.04. Items (1)/(2) still **pending**: no cmd ≤ 0.2 close was run in `t1c_115741`, so the closed-on-object number remains unmeasured (U-5 partial). **Acceptance carried over from R-36, corrected by R-37:** the goal-accept rate depends on span, not a fixed constant — **goal rate ≈ span·2/period/0.02 per side** (the old ≈6×/s-per-side / ≈900-per-150 s figures assumed span 0.3; span 0.7 gives ≈7×/s per side, matching R-37's +1782/150 s); `grip cmd = 0.70` rows read L/R ≈ 0.65–0.66 in R-37 (the map, not 1:1, sets the expected value — see `docs/CONTRACTS.md`); some `GripperCommand_Result` lines show `reached_goal=True` (651 in R-37).
**Status 2026-09-02 end: item (3) confirmed live (R-37); items (1)/(2) PENDING — no cmd ≤ 0.2 close was ever commanded, so the closed-on-object number is still unmeasured (U-5).**

**T5 — open-loop replay of a held-out episode through the runner** (F-82's role on the rig). Entry: T1 with the runner path, T1b's verdict applied as the runner default, arms driven to episode 9's frame 0 (`home_arms.py --file ~/t5_ep009_home_pose.yaml`, a yaml with the same schema as `teleop_home_pose.yaml`, per R-47 — **not** `teleop_home_pose.yaml` itself), then verified with `--start-pose file:outputs/rig/t5/start_pose_s27a15_ep009.json`. Other prerequisites found 2026-09-02: replay must be given as canonical-20 npy + meta (`--adapter replay`, **not** `--action-layout s27a15`); a `--joint-csv` measured-vs-commanded trace (landing); a 30 s `--adapter dummy` dry run of `run_policy --world real` first (never run on this rig yet); cameras either off (`--cameras ''`) or at corpus resolution (U-21); `--seconds 22`. Replay episode 9's recorded actions from frame 0 at 20 Hz with `--max-delta 0.05`, video on. Exit: measured joints track the recorded ones (report the per-joint max error and `clamped_pct`); **21 of episode 9's 341 frames exceed the 0.05 rad/tick clamp (R-47), so `clamped_pct` ≈ 6 % is expected even for a perfect replay** — a higher figure, not a nonzero one, is the fault signal. If it does not track, the fault is calibration/frame/executor and nothing scored afterwards means anything. **Status (2026-09-02): T5.1 dry run PASS (R-52)** — the runner's real-robot choreography (hold → self-activation → 20 Hz loop with keep-alive → self-deactivation → joint trace) works end to end; next **T5.2** = `home_arms.py --file ~/t5_ep009_home_pose.yaml` to episode 9's frame 0 (largest motion of the day: PTP 0.15 rad/s, up to 3.3 rad, into the task-2 configuration — workspace check + E-stop), then **T5.3** the replay with `--start-pose file:outputs/rig/t5/start_pose_s27a15_ep009.json --start-pose-tol 0.10`. **Status (2026-09-02): T5.2 done (R-53)** — episode 9's frame 0 is PTP-reachable, 22 s left / 9 s right, via `home_arms.py --file`; the operator judged the reached left-arm configuration unsafe/awkward for this cell and hand-guided it away (reason pending — U-25). **T5.3 attempted and correctly REFUSED (R-54)** — the left arm was 0.66 rad off pose from that hand-guiding (not a bug), `StartPoseNotReached` fired before activation, nothing was commanded; the right arm was exactly on frame 0. `verify_start_pose` verified both arms regardless of `--arms` at the time of R-54; the fix restricting verification to the driven `--arms` is landing 2026-09-02 (uncommitted, `camelo/runner/real_arms.py`). **Next step:** re-home the left arm to frame 0 (or accept it held) and re-run T5.3 as **`--arms right`** once the fix lands — task-2's grasp is a right-gripper action, so a right-arm-only replay of episode 9 is the T5 fallback, and T5's exit criterion (measured-vs-commanded joint tracking, `clamped_pct`) is then measured on the right arm only. **Status (2026-09-02): T5 DONE on the right arm: mechanism PASS, wrist tracking FAIL → U-26; left arm replay not run (U-25)** (R-55, `t5_replay_ep9_155241`) — the runner's full replay choreography (activation, chunk timing, keep-alive, self-deactivation, joint trace) measured correct, `clamped_pct` 10.3 % in the expected range; j1–j5 tracked within 0.20 rad, but j6/j7 lagged 0.56/0.65 rad under the impedance controller's compliant wrist gains, bounding what T6 can expect from wrist fidelity (**U-26**). The left arm was never re-homed to frame 0 after R-53's hand-guiding, so the two-arm replay `verify_start_pose` was designed to unblock (R-54's fix) has still not itself been run (**U-25**).
**Status 2026-09-02 end: T5 DONE on the right arm — mechanism PASS, wrist tracking FAIL (U-26, a controller slew cap, not a runner bug); two-arm replay UNVERIFIED; the scene the replay ran in was itself wrong until 16:11 (R-58/R-59, C-15).**

**T6 — `run_policy --world real --backend remote`, first rung per 15 §2.1's seeded ranking** (as of merge `8dbd979`: MolmoAct2@012500, 13/22 on both seeds; EO-1 11/22 ties ACT — take the order from 15, do not hardcode a rung here). **Entry criteria — four, none optional, all as of 2026-09-02 end:** **(1) C-15 base view — MET** (R-60, 16:14; re-verify by overlay whenever the base moves, and home the left arm out of frame first). **(2) U-21 wrist shape — CLOSED 2026-09-03 11:35 (R-73)**: both wrist launches land the corpus 640×480 via `config_file:=configs/d405_color_640x480.yml` (not `enable_depth:=true`, which was tried and does not move colour), confirmed by the camera window's own `Open profile … Width: 640` line — **a site-config decision, not ours** — `check_obs` confirmation (exit 1, no SHAPE MISMATCH) is still pending. **(3) U-26 wrist slew — OPEN**: `max_goal_velocity` ≥ 1.0 rad/s on the companion (`controllers.yaml:26`) — **also a site-config decision**; without it a rollout's wrist error is a controller ceiling, not a policy result. **(4) the parity gate 15 §1.2 PASS on the rig box** (`tools/rig_parity_check.py … --episode 9`, then one `--negative-control` with teeth). Plus the blocks themselves: T3 (ingest PASS, shape pending (2)), T4, T5; GPU box serving `serve_policy.py` over a tunnel; keep-alive gap logged; **the start pose for rollouts must be inside the corpus start distribution (R-47), not `teleop_home_pose.yaml`/the task-3 home** — see U-24 for which task-2 pose to use. **Wrist-tracking fidelity is bounded by U-26** (R-55: j6/j7 lagged 0.56/0.65 rad in open-loop replay) — a policy commanding fast wrist motion may not be trackable by this controller regardless of runner/adapter correctness; read rollout wrist error against that ceiling, not zero. **U-26 answered: the mechanism is the controller's `max_goal_velocity: 0.5 rad/s` slew cap, not the gains — wrist fidelity requires `max_goal_velocity ≥ 1.0 rad/s` on the companion (site config change, `controllers.yaml:26`, operator decision) before T6 wrist error can be read as a policy result rather than a controller ceiling.** **The corpus videos must show the robot performing the task (R-56/R-57 check) before any rollout is scored** — confirmed for episode 9 (R-57: the right arm parks above/behind the head camera at frame 0 and swings down to perform the grasp on-camera, no human-demonstration risk), but the same spot-check should be re-run against whichever episode/config actually seeds the T6 start pose (U-24) rather than assumed from episode 9 alone. **U-25's physical-scene risk is closed for the base** (R-58 → R-59 → R-60: the base was ≈1 m off, was repositioned in two push-and-capture iterations, and the live head view now matches corpus frame 0 within a few cm) — what remains is the left arm still parked in frame and today's red pad in slot 3 vs episode 9's slot 4; record the pad slot with every rollout. Protocol and N per 15 §2 (15 rollouts, ≥ 5 per slot at x ≈ 0.48 / 0.51 / 0.61, randomized once), one row per rollout in 15 §2.5's `rollouts.csv`, video every rollout, E-stop every rollout. Exit: 15 rows.
**Status 2026-09-02 end: NOT STARTED** — entry criterion (1) met (R-60), (2) and (3) open and both site-config decisions, (4) never run on the rig box.

**Note (2026-09-02) on T5/T6 image-rate entry:** image rate needed ≈ 4 Hz per camera (replan cadence), not 20; shape parity is the gate (U-21), not ingest Hz (U-22) — see U-23.

## 6. Open questions — UNMEASURED

| # | Question | Why it matters | How to measure |
|---|---|---|---|
| U-1 | Mechanism of the memory growth (R-22/23). Ranked in [16b §1](16b_CONTAINER_DDS_GUIDANCE.md), **re-ranked 2026-09-02 after R-40**: **(0, new, arguably now top-ranked) version-skew deserialization failure on Fast CDR** — R-40 measured 398 `sequence size exceeds remaining buffer` lines (a Fast CDR deserialize error) appear across *every* Humble (2.6.10) node on the companion the moment a Jazzy (2.14.6) participant joins the graph, and stop the moment it leaves; U-9 saw the same string once on 2026-09-01 during `activate_arms.py` discovery. This is the same "every node, all at once, right after a foreign participant joins" shape as R-22/R-23's station-side OOM, now with a primary-source symptom string instead of only a reasoned mechanism, though R-40 was on the *companion*, not the *station*, and produced no growth (only the error string) — it upgrades hypothesis (1) below from reasoned to partially observed, it does not yet reproduce the OOM itself; (1) version-skew retransmission storm on the builtin SEDP endpoints (reasoned, no primary source before R-40); (2) uncapped RELIABLE writer history on a station node fed by a slow-to-ACK reader; camelo-side leaks and GUID/PID collision ruled out for *native* RSS growth. **Further data point 2026-09-02 (R-43, R-45):** a native Humble (pixi, same-version) participant — first one camera worker (24 min), then all three plus the `check_obs` node (301 RSS samples over 25 min, `MEM used` peaked 6163 MB during the 4-participant run and returned to 5690 MB within the 3-min soak) — coexisted with every station camera node at **flat, near-zero RSS growth** throughout. This strengthens hypothesis (0)/(1) (version-skew specific) over any reading of R-22/R-23 as "a foreign participant joins, memory grows" in general: same-version (Humble↔Humble) coexistence is clean; only a Jazzy participant has ever been present at an OOM onset. **Also noted 2026-09-02 (R-48):** the station's own nodes run with the same 208 KB `net.core.rmem_default` receive-buffer default that R-48 found causing camera-frame loss on our participant — a candidate contributor to their behaviour under load; unmeasured, not folded into the ranking above. | Decides whether any container can coexist with the station nodes | T2 steps 3 vs 4 separate (1) from (2); a FastDDS log-level bump on one node; **T2's container step 3 must now also grep the watched station nodes' stderr/logs for `sequence size exceeds remaining buffer`, not just watch RSS** — a version-skew deserialize failure could precede or accompany the memory growth |
| U-2 | Why a container on the same host does not discover the station's native participants (R-24) while the companion is discovered. **Answered for set-up C, 2026-09-02 (R-43, R-45):** a native Humble (pixi) participant on the same host discovers and ingests all three station camera topics — the gap is not a same-host-DDS property in general, it is specific to the Jazzy container. Ranked in 16b §1 for the container case: ~~profile not loaded~~ (ruled out for the on-site bundle as measured; the branch itself lacked the fix until 2026-09-01 evening, now landed), lost `127.0.0.1` (compounding), Fast DDS 2.6.10 ↔ 2.14.6 skew (pinned by R-40: `ros-jazzy-fastrtps 2.14.6` / `ros-jazzy-rmw-fastrtps-cpp 8.4.4` / `fastcdr 2.2.7` vs the station's `ros-humble-fastrtps 2.6.10` / `ros-humble-rmw-fastrtps-cpp 6.2.7`, no longer "unverified"), dual-NIC under `network_mode: host`; QoS ruled out by symptom shape. Services confirmed broken across the Jazzy↔Humble gap (R-40: discovery worked, `switch_controller`/`list_controllers` never answered); topics work across the same gap (R-30b, R-40's own `js_hz`). **The still-open half — whether *any* container (Jazzy set-up A, or the Humble image set-up B) can discover the station cameras — is folded into U-20**, which already owns "does set-up B reach the station cameras"; T2 steps 3–4 (container RSS + discovery) remain the way to answer it for the Jazzy container specifically. | Blocks all camera policies from any container | for set-up C: **done** (R-43/R-45); for A/B: T2 steps 3–5 and U-20 |
| U-3 | ~~Corpus action frame~~ **settled: robot-frame** (§4 decision 2). ~~Still open: was the controller's `dir = [−1,−1,1,1,1,1,−1]` in force when the corpus was recorded, i.e. must the publisher pre-transform joints 1, 2, 7?~~ **Answered (2026-09-02, R-39, `t1bj1_120702`).** No — joint 1 measured **in phase** with an identity publish (corr = +0.922, max \|meas\| 0.0173/0.02 rad), so `dir[0] = −1` was not effectively in force for our stand-in; identity publish is correct and `--arm-command-frame robot` stays the default, now measured rather than assumed. **Why** the controller's own `dir = −1` did not show up here is unmeasured — see U-17. | Decides the `--arm-command-frame` default; a wrong answer swings joints 1/2/7 the wrong way | T1b (j1 ±0.02 rad, phase of measured vs commanded) — **done** |
| U-4 | **Answered (2026-09-02, R-32 vs R-33).** The standing ≈ +0.026 rad offset on j6/j7 after activation (R-30b): activation delta capture, gravity/friction at low k, or both? Answer: **activation delta capture** (g0 ≠ q0 when activation happens mid-wiggle, R-32; zero offset when activation happens inside the hold so g0 = q0, R-33) — not gravity. | Sets the tolerance for start-pose verification and for "tracks" in T5 | activate with the probe at exactly zero phase, compare |

Fix in progress: `camelo/contracts.py`'s `gripper_width_percent` → 0..1, after which `--gripper-command width_percent` is numerically identical to `open_fraction` and no separate wiring is needed. | A unit mismatch was a silent perfectly-timed close that never grips (12 §2 item 3) — confirmed as of R-36 | verify with the next T1(c)/T4 run once the contracts fix lands (acceptance in R-36) |

| U-23 | **CLOSED 2026-09-02 — the guard is in the runner.** `--max-image-age-s` (`camelo/cli.py`, default **0.5 s on `--world real`**, OFF in sim, `0` disables) makes `run_rollout` sample every camera's newest frame at each INFERENCE — beside `ImageAgeMeter.consume`, i.e. at the ~3.75 Hz replan cadence this row derives, not the 20 Hz tick — and raise `StaleImageError` (`camelo/control/image_age.py`) when one is older than the threshold. The age read is the frame's WALL receipt time (`FrameLog.last_wall()` / `ObsCollector.last_image_wall()`), NOT its sim-time age, so the alarm does not depend on the camera's stamp origin agreeing with the clock (`image_drain_age_*` can read negative on this rig). The exception escapes `run_rollout` exactly as `StartPoseNotReached` does: `scripts/run_policy.py`'s guarded `finally` deactivates the arms while still publishing, the fault line is logged at ERROR, and the process exits non-zero. `scripts/check_obs.py --world real` prints the same line. Offline coverage: `tests/test_stale_image_guard.py` (fresh passes, stale raises at the first inference, never-delivered raises, guard-off never raises, real/sim defaults). The original finding, unchanged: **New 2026-09-02, before T6.** No code gates `run_policy`/the runner on image AGE or rate: `get_obs` (`camelo/ros/obs_collector.py:461-486`) returns as soon as every camera key has *some* array, never checking how stale it is, and `self.images[key]` (set at `obs_collector.py:267`, initialised at `:79`) is never cleared once a first frame lands — so a camera that dies mid-rollout (process crash, DDS drop, the R-22/R-23 OOM class) keeps serving its last decoded frame forever, silently, with no fault line. This is orthogonal to the ingest-rate findings above (U-21/U-22): even a camera at the correct shape and rate needs a liveness guard, because the failure mode here is total loss, not degradation. Also relevant to why 8–15 Hz ingest (U-21/U-22, pre-fix) was never actually a T5/T6 blocker: the rollout ticks at 20 Hz but `ChunkExecutor` only requests a new chunk every `replan_after_steps = 8` steps (`camelo/control/chunk_executor.py:82`) at dt = 1/30 s → inference at 3.75 Hz (2.5 Hz for a 20 Hz-clock checkpoint), and `ImageAgeMeter.consume` samples the image age once per inference call, not once per tick (`episode_runner.py:564-565`) — so the images a policy actually consumes need only keep up with ~4 Hz, not 20 Hz; the real gate on T3 acceptance was shape parity (U-21), not the ingest-rate numbers. | A camera dying mid-rollout would feed the policy a frozen frame indefinitely with no error, fault line, or exit code to catch it — silent, not loud, exactly the failure class AGENTS.md's drift-alarm rule exists to prevent; must be closed before T6 puts a policy in the loop unattended | add an image-age check to `get_obs`/`wait_for_obs` (age vs `sim_time`, threshold set by the ~4 Hz replan cadence, not the 20 Hz tick) that refuses/faults on a stale frame instead of silently reusing it; land it as a `run_policy --world real` guard analogous to the existing camera-shape-mismatch refusal |

It now runs on its OWN daemon thread with a monotonic schedule (`camelo/ros/command_publisher.py`, `_keepalive_loop`), so neither the executor nor a blocked main thread can slow it; `close()` joins it, `--keepalive-hz 0` still disables it with the existing warning. the chunk never reaches the executor or the wire, so the rollout's first chunk still starts at its own t_sim 0, and a dead policy server now ends the run before activation. `warmup_infer_s` is reported; pinned by `test_the_held_arm_is_restamped_on_every_publish` / `test_a_keepalive_repeat_restamps_both_sides`). The ages are a **queue depth**: `create_publisher(..., DEFAULT_DEPTH=10)` is KEEP_LAST(10) RELIABLE, and 10 samples of a 19.95 Hz stream is 0.50 s — exactly the reject limit — so a reader that stalls briefly is handed a backlog whose oldest entry is already at the limit rather than simply missing it. Real arm command publishers are now depth **1** (`ARM_CMD_DEPTH_REAL`, newest-wins; depth is not part of QoS compatibility so it cannot unmatch the companion, and sim keeps `DEFAULT_DEPTH`). | U-31 | **CLOSED in code 2026-09-03, UNMEASURED on the rig — async chunk splice.** In async mode an arriving chunk was installed at `(t_sim − t0)/dt` (≈ 9–11 of 21 at the measured 0.28–0.5 s round trip), which presumes the arm executed the previous chunk's elapsed steps in full; under the 0.05 rad/tick clamp and the companion's 0.5 rad/s slew cap it did not, so every arrival was a maximal (clamped) jump and the arm wiggled at the chunk cadence (R-68: **70/70 arrivals at the clamp**, re-verified from `t6_act_093858.csv` — at every tick with a non-empty `infer_ms`, max abs(Δcmd) over the seven driven joints = 0.0500 exactly; non-arrival ticks average 0.0267). | The first real rollout's failure to descend/close cannot be attributed to the checkpoint while the executor injects a 2 Hz disturbance of its own; every scored rollout would carry it. | Implemented in `ChunkExecutor` (`camelo/control/chunk_executor.py`), selected by `--chunk-splice {index,nearest,offset}` + `--splice-ramp-ticks` (default 8), resolved by `camelo.cli.chunk_splice_from_args` to **`nearest` whenever `--async-inference` is on** and `index` otherwise — so every synchronous and sim result stays byte-identical. `nearest` = start the new chunk at the **fractional** index in `[max(0, wall_idx − horizon), horizon − 1]` whose target is closest (L2 over the 14 arm dims — what `step` clamps) to the CURRENT commanded target, stored as a per-chunk index OFFSET so playback still advances at real time; whole-row rounding was rejected because half a chunk step is several times the clamp here and R-68's jump would have survived it. `offset` = keep the wall index and add `(current_cmd − chunk[wall_idx])` to the arm targets, decaying linearly to zero over `--splice-ramp-ticks` COMMAND ticks. Grippers/base are never corrected. Two invariants: `needs_replan` is judged on the **wall** index (a rewind does not refresh the observation, and judging it on playback would walk the replacement index past the horizon into starvation), `exhausted` on the **spliced** index. Telemetry in `ExecutorStats`/rollout stats and on each `policy chunk #N` line (`splice=-6.0 jump=0.012`): `arrival_jump_rad_mean`/`_max` (pre-clamp), `splice_shift_mean`/`_max`, `spliced_arrivals` + raw sums. Tests: `tests/test_chunk_splice.py` (fake 40 %-speed arm, 21-row chunks, a reply every 9 ticks — `index` hands the arm the full clamp at every arrival, `nearest`/`offset` ≤ 0.2× it, `nearest` reaches the goal sooner with 0 direction flips vs 36). **STILL TO DO: re-measure R-67's setup on the rig** and compare `clamped_pct`, direction flips and hover p2p; docs/CONTRACTS.md §"Where an arriving chunk resumes". | **Review note 2026-09-03 (GO):** `nearest` may splice FORWARD (shift > 0) when the command has run ahead of a chunk computed from a stale observation; the reviewer's proposed bound to `wall_idx + 1` was tried and reverted — in the slow-robot test it reintroduces a 0.53 rad jump, because there the forward pick IS the continuous hand-over. Forward picks cost horizon (hold begins at shift ≥ +2 at the measured RTT) and degrade to `hold` (last target repeated, never extrapolated) while `needs_replan` still fires on the wall index. Watch `splice=` on the `policy chunk` lines and `starved_ticks`; also: the gripper/base columns are read at the spliced row too (no correction term, but a time shift), inert for ACT whose gripper channel was flat in R-67, and `--grasp-gate` overrides downstream. **Measured 2026-09-03 14:51–14:58 (R-83): `offset` (async, observation-time base, `--splice-ramp-ticks 20`) is the best of the three splices measured on this rig** — `starved_ticks` 0 and `clamped_pct` ≤ 1 % on 3 of 4 runs, L2 to the demo's grasp pose down to 0.08 rad on the 120 s repeat (`t6a_a02_145428`), vs `nearest` (U-32)'s leash-bound sawtooth and `index`'s clamped jumps. `nearest` has not been re-tested since R-83; `offset`'s additive correction (no index search, no `argmin`) is the more likely reason it avoids `nearest`'s forward-splice/hold failure mode noted in this review, not a re-measurement of the same mechanism. |

(i) **The anchor.** The policy's chunk is anchored at the MEASURED state it observed (row 0 ≈ measured pose, small per-row steps), while U-31's `nearest` searched for the last COMMANDED target, which leads the measured pose by the tracking lag (the companion's `joint_impedance_controller` slews at 0.5 rad/s, half our 0.05 rad/tick clamp) — so the closest row sits near the chunk's END → `exhausted` → `hold`. Under `index` (R-67/R-70) the same lead produced a maximal clamped jump on every arrival instead. (ii) **Near-ties.** In the same trace the lead was actually SMALL (abs(cmd − measured) mean 0.05, p90 0.12, max 0.28 rad) precisely BECAUSE the executor held 70 % of the time, so (i) alone does not explain +11..+15: when the policy hovers, the chunk's rows sit within float noise of each other and `argmin` lands on an arbitrary far row. Both symptoms have one root: **the command is allowed to run ahead of the pose the arm actually holds.** | Every scored rollout carries it. Under `index` the executor injects a ~2 Hz disturbance of its own (U-31); under `nearest` it hands the arm almost nothing at all — 413 held ticks is a policy that was never executed, and the R-67/R-70 verdict "approach, then hover" cannot be attributed to the checkpoint while either is true. Grippers and the base are never leashed (an open fraction and a VELOCITY). (2) **The anchor**: `nearest` now searches from the MEASURED arm pose of the most recent `step` (stored in the executor), bounded forward to `wall_idx + 2` rows (`NEAREST_FORWARD_ROWS`), with near-ties — distances within `NEAREST_TIE_EPS_RAD` = 1e-3 of the minimum — resolved toward the WALL index rather than toward the earliest/farthest row. `index`/`offset` semantics, the `needs_replan`-on-wall-index and `exhausted`-on-spliced-index invariants, and the arrival-jump metric (still measured against the last COMMAND, so it stays comparable with R-68's 70/70) are all unchanged; with no measured state yet seen the pre-U-32 behaviour stands. **Telemetry**: `leash_active_ticks`, `leash_active_pct`, `cmd_lead_rad_max` (max abs(cmd − measured) over the 14 arm joints, after the leash — recorded whether the leash is on or off) in `ExecutorStats`/rollout stats, `leash_rad` beside them so a 0 % cannot be misread as "never needed", `lead=` on every `policy chunk #N` log line, and a `leash telemetry:` line at the end of `run_policy.py`. **Tests** (no ROS): `tests/test_command_leash.py` — a fake companion that closes 50 % of the error per tick under a 0.5 rad/s slew cap, 21-row chunks, a reply every 9 ticks: the command never exceeds measured ± leash, the arm still reaches the goal, splice shifts stay ≤ +2 with `starved_ticks` 0 over a 10 s run at a 0.45 s RTT, a hovering chunk splices at the wall index (and a tie-blind `argmin` on the same chunk picks row 15 of 21 — the rig's mechanism, reproduced), and the sim/synchronous path is untouched with the leash off. `tests/test_chunk_splice.py`'s three `nearest` cases were re-pointed at the new anchor (the arm-side hand-over gap is now 0.05 rad vs `index`'s 0.97 in that fake); its `index`/`offset` cases are unchanged. **Re-measured on the rig 2026-09-03 10:55 (R-72, `t6_act_105457`, defaults `--chunk-splice nearest` + `--leash-rad 0.15`):** `starved_ticks` 16 of 579 (not 413 — the hold is gone), splice shifts back to R-71's magnitude on the early chunks (−12.9, −8.0) settling to the `wall_idx + 2` bound (+1.2…+2.0) on later, near-stationary chunks, `cmd_lead_rad_max` 0.150 and `leash_active_pct` 19.9 % (115/579) exactly at the configured leash. The predicted "≪ 0.05" on `arrival_jump_rad_max` did NOT materialize: mean 0.150 / max 0.292 rad, because the leash itself is now the mechanism producing the jump — the command runs `leash_rad` ahead of the lagging measured pose and every arrival re-anchors it there, a sawtooth at chunk cadence instead of R-68's 2 Hz clamp-jump. `clamped_pct` 28.0 %, hover peak-to-peak back up near R-67's range (0.04–0.23 rad) because the arm is being driven again, not held. **Honest residual:** the leash is a following limit, so an arm that genuinely cannot move (a fault, a collision, `max_goal_velocity` too low — U-26) will now also stop the command advancing rather than winding up ahead of it; that is the intent, but it means a high `leash_active_pct` is a signal about the CONTROLLER, not about the policy. The measured anchor is also one tick stale by construction (`set_chunk` runs before `step` on the arrival tick), which costs at most one tick of arm motion. **Re-measured again 2026-09-03 11:29 (R-74, `t6_act_112859`) after U-26's `max_goal_velocity` was raised to 1.0 rad/s and the wrist resolution fixed (U-21/R-73) — the sawtooth persists at the higher cap**: `arrival_jump_rad` mean 0.142 / max 0.291 rad (R-72: 0.150/0.292, essentially unchanged), `leash_active_pct` 10.2 % (60/587, down from R-72's 19.9 % now that the arm can follow faster), `cmd_lead_rad_max` still exactly 0.15 (the configured leash), hover peak-to-peak up to 0.33 rad on some joints. **So the leash mechanism was never gated by U-26**: raising the velocity cap let the arm keep up more of the time (fewer leash-active ticks) but did not remove the chunk-cadence re-anchoring itself — R-74's chunk dump (U-33) shows the actual mechanism is upstream of the leash, in how each new chunk's row 0 relates to the measured pose it was computed from. docs/CONTRACTS.md §"Where an arriving chunk resumes" + the `--leash-rad` row in "Real-robot run flags". |

U-34's corpus check adds the missing half of this picture: the chunk is centred on the observed state (chunk-mean ≈ state, ≈0.01 rad), not anchored at it, so `nearest`'s pull toward row 0 — not the checkpoint — explains the wrong-direction offset, and the immediate single-chunk fix is `--chunk-splice index` (R-75) ahead of ensembling. **PARKED 2026-09-03**: `--chunk-splice ensemble` was implemented by an agent on a stale base (worktree branch `worktree-agent-a9bd7ec4049df7049`, commit `c00d6a4`, NOT merged) and measured in the fake-robot test only — at the deployed horizon (H=21, dt 0.05 s, 0.45 s RTT, `--replan-steps 8`) only 1.3 chunks cover any one tick, so the "average" is the single chunk on ≈70 % of ticks; `arrival_jump_rad` roughly halves vs `index` (0.146 → 0.071) but hover peak-to-peak is unchanged. A 41-row horizon (3.3 chunks/tick) does what ensembling is supposed to do — jump 0.100 → 0.017 — but that horizon does not exist on the deployed checkpoint. Not a lever for today: needs either a longer chunk (retrain) or a faster round trip, neither available on this branch. |

| U-40 | **SUPERSEDED 2026-09-03 15:18 by U-41 — the transport trim (client resize + camera subset) landed but did not fix it, because the bottleneck was never the payload.** VLA-JEPA's action chunk is 7 rows (0.35 s) at 20 Hz, shorter than the measured 0.37 s server round trip (steady-state; first call 2.5 s warm-up). Under a synchronous plan-then-execute loop (v00) the whole 7-row chunk plays out in ≈0.14 s wall-clock while the next inference has not returned, so the arm ends up holding on the last row of a very short, high-curvature chunk and jumps hard at the next arrival. Under async with the arrival-time base (v01), every chunk starves before the next one arrives and the arrival-time splice re-commands the arm to a pose it already passed one round trip ago — the operator's "moves, moves back to an earlier joint state on the same trajectory, moves again." | VLA-JEPA cannot be scored on this rig with the current transport: the round trip must shrink below the 7-row/0.35 s chunk (or the chunk must lengthen) before a rollout says anything about the checkpoint rather than the transport. Unlike U-35 (ACT), this is not an off-distribution-observation problem — the wire probe already shows ≤ 0.04 rad row-0 agreement with the demo at the recorded frame. | **Done, and superseded (R-84):** client-side resize to 224×224 + two cameras cut the payload from 117 kB to 17 kB, but RTT only dropped 0.24 s → 0.225 s — the round trip is network latency plus server compute, not payload size, and it still exceeds the 0.35 s chunk. v02/v03 (offset splice, both time bases) still wandered/crawled.
